# Mastande ZA — Pre-Launch Checklist

> **Everything that needs a human, a credential or a production deploy is in
> [docs/OUTSTANDING.md](docs/OUTSTANDING.md)**, with the exact command for each
> and how to tell it worked. That file is the one to read before a launch; this
> one is the record of what was found and fixed.

## Launch readiness summary (v1.6.0)

**Corrected 26 Aug 2026.** The previous version of this summary claimed
"everything fixable inside this repo is done… each verified before commit."
That was written before the project had ever been executed. Those pre-commit
checks only proved that imports resolved and exported names matched — they
never ran the code. When it was first run end to end, roughly twenty real
defects surfaced that this checklist did not list:

- `@angular/vitest` — a package that does not exist on npm
- Five dependency majors incompatible with their own cores (TypeScript,
  vitest, `@nestjs/config`/`jwt`/`passport`/`swagger`)
- An SSR crash on every page load (`IntersectionObserver` during server render)
- Three services that aborted API bootstrap when optional credentials were
  absent (Resend, Google OAuth, and `esModuleInterop` for compression)
- A cross-user data leak: saved rooms shared one global storage key, so on a
  shared device one account saw another's saved rooms
- Applications had no letting cycle, and a unique constraint that locked a
  tenant out of a room permanently after one application
- Placeholder ImageKit URLs (`mastande-dev`) that 404'd every image
- The visual spec was never wired up: 15 of 132 class names matched, so the
  design in `RentBoard-ZA-Visual-Preview.html` had never actually applied

**Treat this document as a map of known gaps, not as evidence of working
software.** The distinction matters: it is the reason the above went unnoticed.

### What is now genuinely verified

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Automated backend tests | `npm run test:backend` — 91 unit tests (pure logic, no database). `npm run test:int` — 137 integration tests over the real Nest app, the real guards and an isolated migrated database (`mastande_integration_test`), doubles at the four network edges only. Every spec falsified by reintroducing the defect it exists to catch |
| API end to end | `./scripts/smoke-test.sh` — 64 checks: auth, room lifecycle, applications, messaging, both dashboards, listing wizard validation |
| Cross-tenant isolation | Four IDOR checks: a second tenant cannot read, post into, accept or list another tenant's application |
| ImageKit | `./scripts/test-imagekit.sh` — live credentials, signed upload confirmed |
| Full UI flow | Manually walked: register → post room → upload photo → publish → browse → apply → message |
| Responsive | Ported from `Visual-Preview-v2.html` (900/768/480), verified in device emulation |

### What is still unverified

- No deployment has been done — staging and production are untested
- No automated test coverage beyond one pipe spec; the smoke test is an
  integration check against a running server, not a unit suite
- Email is unconfigured, so the apply → notify → respond loop has never
  completed with a real message reaching a landlord
- Load, backup and restore have never been exercised

The code rename is done — these three are provider-side and nothing in the repo can detect them | Yes — staging's API host and the production domain do not resolve until they are done |
| **Legal filing** | PAIA manual must be filed with the SAHRC (Form 2, no fee) | Yes — legal requirement |

Still rendering live in the footer as `© 2026 [YOUR COMPANY NAME] (Pty) Ltd · CIPC Reg No. [NUMBER]`. The rebrand does not change these — the registered entity is whatever CIPC says, not the trading name | Yes — have an attorney review while you're at it |
| **GitHub settings** | Branch protection rules to actually enforce the squash-merge policy `CONTRIBUTING.md` describes | Recommended, not launch-blocking |

| ~~**KYC integration**~~ | ✅ **Closed in v1.56.0, by not doing it.** The Renter's Passport is no longer payment-only — it is no longer payment at all. A third-party KYC provider was the wrong answer for this market: it screens on a credit history most renters here do not have, and would exclude exactly the people the product is for. Replaced with informal-economy proofs reviewed by a person — SASSA confirmation, employer confirmation, three months of banking-app screenshots, or a previous landlord phoned. See README §22 | No |
| **Translation** | 8 of 11 official languages are honest English-fallback stubs | No — English works, switcher just shouldn't claim those 8 yet |

Full detail on every row above is in the tables below — this summary is the map, not the whole territory.

---

Consolidates every `[PLACEHOLDER]`, deferred item, and audit finding scattered across `README.md §1–19` and your `RentBoard-ZA-Audit-Report.html`, in one place.

A caution on the tables below: "already fixed" in the original text meant the
code had been read, not run. Where a row has not been re-confirmed against a
running system since 26 Aug 2026, treat it as unverified.

Legal counts as much as code here: several of these are compliance-critical, not just security-critical.

## 🔴 Critical — must fix before launch

| # | Item | Status | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ~~JWT stored in `localStorage`~~ | ✅ **Fixed in v0.9.2** | Access token now lives only in an in-memory Angular signal, never persisted. Confirmed by grep — zero `localStorage` references for tokens/user data anywhere in the frontend. |
| 2 | ~~No refresh token rotation~~ | ✅ **Fixed in v0.9.2** | Access token shortened to 15 min. New `RefreshToken` model — opaque random value, stored only as a sha256 hash, delivered as an httpOnly+secure+sameSite cookie, rotated on every use. Reuse of an already-rotated token (theft signal) revokes every session for that user, not just documented as a risk. See `README §20`. |
| 3 | ~~No input sanitisation on free-text fields~~ | ✅ **Fixed in v0.9.1** | `sanitizeText()` (strips all markup) now applied at every write: `Room.title`/`description`, `Application.coverNote`/rejection `reason`, `Message.body` (both in-app and inbound WhatsApp). **Also fixed a sharper version of the same bug found while tracing this:** every `NotificationsService` email template interpolated user text directly into raw HTML with zero escaping — the actual exploitable path, since mail clients render HTML by default. `escapeHtml()` is now applied to every dynamic value in all 6 templates. |
| 4 | ~~Missing PAIA manual route~~ | ✅ **Already fixed** | Built in pass 0.3.0 — `frontend/src/app/features/legal/paia/paia.ts`, routed at `/legal/paia` |

## 🟡 Important — fix before launch

| # | Item | Status | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | **Git squash-merge not enforced** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` documents the policy, but nothing enforces it. This is a GitHub branch-protection setting, not something fixable in files | ⬜ Open — manual GitHub setup | Repo Settings → Branches → `develop`/`main` protection rules |
| 6 | ~~Rebase-before-PR not documented in README~~ | ✅ **Not applicable — already present** | `README.md §7` has documented "rebase-before-PR" in prose and a working `git rebase origin/develop` command example since pass 0.1.0. Re-checked against the actual file rather than assumed. |
| 7 | **Admin guard not wired at module level** | ✅ **Not applicable yet** | No `AdminModule`/admin endpoints exist in this repo at all — nothing to leave unguarded. Flag this again the moment an admin module is built, not before. |
| 8 | ~~`ZarCentsPipe` not imported in components~~ | ✅ **Already fixed** | Used consistently in `RoomCard`, `RoomDetail`, `LandlordDashboard`, `TenantDashboard` since pass 0.3.0/0.4.0 — verified by grep, not assumed |
| 9 | Dashboards not fully mobile-responsive | ⚠️ **Superseded — re-done in v1.3.2–v1.4.3** | The v0.9.3 fix (component `styles: []` with a 480px breakpoint) was itself the problem: Angular's emulated encapsulation makes component styles *more specific* than global CSS, so they silently overrode the entire responsive layer and every breakpoint was ignored. Those styles were removed and both dashboards rebuilt on a shared `PortalShell`. Responsive rules now live in `_responsive.scss`, ported from `Visual-Preview-v2.html` (900/768/480). |
| 12 | **The Lighthouse gate has never been green** — it had run twice, both red. Config faults fixed in v1.58.0 (two `"//1"`/`"//2"` comment keys inside `assertions`, where every key is an audit id; `render-blocking-resources` asserted with `maxNumericValue`, which reads NaN on an opportunity audit; and `"preset": "perf"`, which meant accessibility, best-practices and SEO were never collected at all, so every assertion naming them had no data). The four real findings were fixed in v1.72.0, robots.txt and the soft 404s in v1.73.0–v1.73.1. **Measured on a GitHub runner at v1.73.1, 4 URLs × 3 runs:** performance **0.94** (was 0.84, floor 0.9), accessibility **1.0** (was 0.96–0.98), SEO **1.0** with no failing audits (was 0.92 — `robots-txt` and `is-crawlable` both pass now), LCP **2.16s** (was 3.47s, budget 2.5s), TBT 172ms, CLS 0.035. **One assertion still fails and it is not ours to fix:** best-practices sits at 0.96 on `/` because `errors-in-console` records two `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` entries for `https://api.umastande.co.za` — the board's own listing and ad requests, against a domain that is not registered yet. It clears itself the week the domain resolves. Do not lower the floor to make it green sooner. **api.umastande.co.za resolves and answers as of 27 September 2026**, so the two `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` entries that held this at 0.96 should be gone — re-run the audit to confirm rather than assuming it | ⚠️ **Re-run now that the API resolves** | `lighthouserc.json`, `frontend/src/server.ts`, `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs` |
| 13 | **Dependency advisories.** `npm audit` reported 10 in production dependencies (1 moderate, 9 high). Patch bumps in v1.63.0 cleared `qs`, `js-yaml`, `@nestjs/swagger`, `prisma` and `@prisma/config` — all within the same major, smoke suite and refund drive unchanged. **The 8 that remain are not on the request path, which is why they are here and not above:** four are `multer` DoS advisories, and this app has no `FileInterceptor` and no `@UploadedFile` anywhere — photos go client-side to ImageKit under a signed token, so multer never processes a request; the rest are the NestJS packages that depend on it, plus `deepmerge-ts`, which arrives through the Prisma **CLI's** config loader rather than anything serving traffic. **Cleared in v1.76.0, and neither major upgrade was needed** — that sentence was wrong when it was written and wronger since. The four multer advisories are fixed in `@nestjs/platform-express@11.2.6`, a patch release inside the same major (multer 2.2.0 → 2.4.0), not in NestJS 12. And Prisma 8 does not exist: the latest release is 7.10.0 and the advisory range covers all of 6.13+ and all of 7.x, so upgrading could not have fixed it either. What did: a scoped `overrides` entry pinning `deepmerge-ts` to ^8.0.2 under `@prisma/config`, since Prisma pins 7.1.5 and the stack-exhaustion fix landed in 8.0.0. `npm audit` reports **0 vulnerabilities**, and the change is driven rather than assumed — `prisma validate`, `generate` and `migrate deploy` all pass, and the smoke suite is 327 passed, 0 failed against it. NestJS 12 remains undone on purpose: with the security reason gone, a major framework upgrade before launch is risk bought for nothing | ✅ **0 advisories** | `backend/package.json`, `backend/package-lock.json` |
| 14 | **The deployment check had never completed once.** Nineteen runs, all stuck in the same step until GitHub killed them at six hours — six hours of runner time per push to report nothing. Four faults: no `--max-time` on any curl (a suspended Render service accepts the connection and never answers, so one call froze the loop), no job `timeout-minutes`, a "not deployed yet" guard that tested DNS when every `*.onrender.com` name resolves whether or not a service exists, and a hostname pointing at a service that was never created. Rewritten in v1.65.0 and driven through eight scenarios including the original hang — old script exit 124 still running at 90s, new script exit 1 at 78s with a diagnosis. It now also asserts the deployed **version matches the pushed commit**, so a pass means the deploy landed rather than that something answered | ✅ **Fixed — needs `STAGING_API_HOST` set to start enforcing** | `.github/workflows/verify-deployment.yml`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` §5 |
| 15 | **Staging silently stopped deploying for five releases, and CI never noticed.** v1.62.0 added `postinstall: prisma generate` to `backend/package.json` — right for a developer's checkout, fatal in the Dockerfile's production stage, which runs `npm ci --only=production` where the prisma CLI is a devDependency and therefore absent. npm exits 127, the image never builds, and Render goes on serving the last good build. Staging sat at v1.61.0 through v1.66.0 while every CI run stayed green, because **nothing in CI had ever built `backend/Dockerfile`** — the backend job runs `nest build` on a full install, which is a different path. Fixed in v1.67.0: `--omit=dev --ignore-scripts` in that stage (it needs no scripts — the Prisma client is copied from the builder, and bcrypt ships prebuilt binaries), plus a CI job that builds the image Render builds and boots it | ✅ **Fixed** | `backend/Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` |
| 17 | **Nine nav items went nowhere, and two named things that do not exist.** Clicking every item in both portals rather than reading the route table: `My Rooms`, `Applicants`, `Applications`, `Saved Rooms` and `Alerts` all navigated to the dashboard root — and from the dashboard that is indistinguishable from a click that did nothing, even though every one of them names a real section further down the same page. `Messages` in both portals named a screen that has never existed; threads live inside an application. Fixed in v1.70.0: fragments on the section items (`anchorScrolling` was already enabled, the items just never used it), verified by measuring `scrollY` after each click, and `Messages` marked `disabled` so it renders as a greyed “Soon” chip instead of a promise | ✅ **Fixed** | landlord + tenant `navItems`, `portal-shell.ts` |
| 16 | **Two shipped screens had no way to reach them.** `/landlord/yard` had no navigation entry at all, and `/tenant/passport`'s entry was still wrapped in `BILLING_ENABLED` — correct when the Passport sold an R89/month subscription, wrong from the moment Phase 1 made it free, and `BILLING_ENABLED` is false. The routes were fine and both pages rendered when typed into the address bar, which is exactly why a route-level check found nothing. A user clicking around could not get to either. Fixed in v1.69.0 | ✅ **Fixed** | landlord + tenant dashboard `navItems` |
| 18 | **Room pages were never actually server-rendered.** `/rooms/:id` is `RenderMode.Server` precisely so a crawler sees the room, and it served `Loading…` with the site's default title, the site's default description and no JSON-LD — on every room page, for every crawler, since the interceptor was written. `authInterceptor` returned `EMPTY` for *every* request to our own API during SSR, public reads included: `EMPTY` completes without emitting, so neither the `next` nor the `error` handler ran and the page serialised in its loading state. Three consequences, all measured: the prerendered board came out of the build showing its empty state; a room id that does not exist rendered its 200 rather than its not-found branch; and the room page's `aggregateRating` could never appear because the data it reads was never fetched. Fixed in v1.73.0 — `SSR_PUBLIC_PREFIXES` lets `/rooms`, `/reviews`, `/places` and `/ads` GETs through on the server and nothing else. A real room page went from 18,663 bytes of shell to 29,167 bytes with the title, description, room copy and `Product`/`Offer`/`AggregateRating` JSON-LD all in the served HTML | ✅ **Fixed and asserted** | `auth.interceptor.ts`, `scripts/verify-build.sh` |
| 19 | **The site's origin had no robots.txt and no sitemap.xml.** Both are generated by the backend — on the API's origin, where no crawler of the site will ever read them. At the site's own origin both paths fell through to the router's catch-all and answered **200 with the "Page not found" page**, so every `Disallow` was inert and the sitemap URL submitted to Search Console was a 200 with an HTML body. Lighthouse had been reporting it all along: `robots-txt` returned "58 errors found", each a line of our 404 page it could not parse, which is the single audit that held SEO at 0.92 on all four URLs in CI. Fixed in v1.73.0 in both places that answer that URL: a five-minute-cached proxy in `frontend/src/server.ts` (development, `node server.mjs`, the Lighthouse job) and edge rewrites in `frontend/vercel.json` (production, which is static). When the API is unreachable the fallback answers from the build's own `environment.indexable`: production stays crawlable, staging and development disallow. **The first version answered `Disallow: /` on any failure** — which is not the cautious direction but the destructive one, since a missing robots.txt means "crawl freely" while that line is an instruction Google obeys. It made the score it was meant to fix worse (SEO 0.92 → 0.66, `is-crawlable` 0) and the content-type assertion I had just added passed it. Both directions are asserted now, each against the build it belongs to | ✅ **Fixed and asserted** | `frontend/src/server.ts`, `frontend/vercel.json`, `docs/SEO.md` |
| 20 | **Every unknown URL answered HTTP 200.** The catch-all was `RenderMode.Prerender`, and a prerendered route cannot carry a status. So `/anything` was a soft 404 — which keeps the URL in the index, competing with real pages, and is what made `/robots.txt` answer 200 with HTML (row 19). Two halves to the fix, because one is not enough: the catch-alls now carry `status: 404`, and — since a `:lang` server route matches *any* single segment, server route matching happening before `localeMatchGuard` can rule it out — `/foo` and `/xx/pricing` never reach a catch-all at all. Those get their status from a marker the not-found page renders about itself, mapped in `server.ts`. `/rooms` is a declared 301. A room that does not exist answers 404 from the same marker | ✅ **Fixed and asserted** | `app.routes.ts`, `error-page.ts`, `room-detail.ts`, `frontend/src/server.ts` |
| 21 | **The portal navigation was defined six times and the copies disagreed.** The landlord dashboard listed eight items, `/landlord/yard` four, `/landlord/verification` two; the tenant dashboard nine, `/tenant/rent` five, `/tenant/passport` two; `/account/settings` had a third, three-item version per role. So the sidebar *shrank* as a person moved through their own portal: from the verification screen a landlord could not reach their property or their settings, and a tenant who opened their Renter's Passport lost Rent, Browse rooms, Settings, Applications, Saved rooms and Alerts. The admin side already did this correctly with one `ADMIN_NAV` constant — that pattern now covers all three roles, and `ADMIN_NAV` gained the Settings entry it never had (an admin had no way to reach `/account/settings` from any admin screen). `scripts/phase-drive.mjs` compares the rendered sidebar across four screens per role | ✅ **Fixed and asserted** | `landlord-nav.ts`, `tenant-nav.ts`, `admin-nav.ts`, `scripts/phase-drive.mjs` |
| 22 | **The room page said nothing about who was letting the room.** The board's room card showed a `✓ Verified` badge; the page a tenant reads before sending a stranger their details showed no badge, no name and no rating — and the detail endpoint did not even send the landlord, so it could not have. Phase 1's premise is that a badge should have a visible basis, and the basis was missing from the one screen where the decision is made. Fixed in v1.73.0: `PUBLIC_LANDLORD` is one selection shared by the list and the detail query (name, avatar, rating, `idVerified` — no email, no phone, no document), and the page states the verification either way, because silence reads as "fine" and most listings will be unverified for a while yet | ✅ **Fixed** | `rooms.service.ts`, `room-detail.ts` |
| 23 | **A local database behind on migrations reports as 110 product failures.** `scripts/smoke-test.sh` failed 110 checks with `401 Authentication required` everywhere, which reads as a broken auth system; the cause was `users.openFlagCount` not existing, so registration 500d and every authenticated check after it had no session. One `npx prisma migrate deploy` took it to 405 passed, 0 failed. This is the same shape as the remote run that reported 109 failures — **check migrations before reading a smoke run as a product fault.** `docs/RUNBOOK.md` now leads with it | ⚠️ **Environment, not code — documented** | `docs/RUNBOOK.md` |
| 24 | **Production had no SSR, and then had no site at all.** Two settings, both wrong, both live on 22 September: `outputDirectory: .../browser` published the client bundle and discarded `server.mjs`, so the deployment was static and its SPA fallback (pointing at `/index.html`, which `cleanUrls` makes unreachable) 404'd **every deep link** — `/auth/login`, the portals, every room link ever shared. Removing the setting was worse: Vercel read `outputPath` from `angular.json`, published `dist/mastande-frontend`, and **the whole site 404'd** including `/`, until it was reverted. Neither value is right, because the Angular preset does not understand the `browser/` + `server/` split. Fixed in v1.75.0 with `frontend/tools/vercel-build.mjs`, which assembles Vercel's Build Output API — static assets, an `ssr.func` carrying `server.mjs` and the browser bundle, and routes that send every page to it. `verify-build.sh` assembles the artefact, checks its shape and routes, **boots the function entry and asks it for three URLs**. Also found by booting it: Angular 21 answers **400**, not a client-rendered fallback, to a Host it does not recognise — so `server.ts` now trusts the hostnames the platform itself provides (`VERCEL_URL` and friends), which is what stops every preview deployment being half-broken **Verified in production on 27 September 2026**, against www.umastande.co.za with the API live: a room page answers **200** with `"@type":"Product"` in the served HTML (it had answered 503 since the domain existed, because the API was unreachable); `/robots.txt` answers `Allow: /` from the site's own origin with the private-area section intact; `/sitemap.xml` answers `application/xml; charset=utf-8`. That is the first time this application has server-rendered for a real visitor | ✅ **Live and measured** | `frontend/tools/vercel-build.mjs`, `frontend/vercel.json`, `frontend/src/server.ts`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md §4` |
| 25 | **Every production build had failed for three days, and four releases were tagged over the red.** CI has run on `master` and `develop` since v1.68.0, and it went red at **v1.73.0 and stayed red for ten consecutive runs** — v1.73.0, v1.73.1, v1.74.0, v1.74.1, v1.75.0 and v1.75.1 were all cut on a failing run, and the `develop → master` merge carried it to `master` unchanged. Two jobs, and neither failure was what it looked like. **The frontend job's production build** reported `An error occurred while prerendering route '/af'` and `Terminating worker thread` for five more routes; the cause was one line further up and only in the full log: `AbortError: Request for: http://localhost:39047/af was aborted. TimeoutError`. `@angular/build` aborts a route at 30s and tears down the worker pool, so one hanging request fails the **whole build** and names five innocent routes. The hanging request was the board's own listing call: v1.73.0 made public reads real during SSR (row 18) and nothing bounded the wait, and `api.umastande.co.za` resolves on a GitHub runner to something that accepts the connection and never answers. Reproduced locally in 43s against a socket that accepts and never replies, then fixed and re-driven: build succeeds, and a room page answers **503 in 8.3s** instead of never. Fixed with `serverTimeoutInterceptor` — 8s on server-side requests to our own API, raising `504` so it lands on the same branch every component already handles. **A refused API had always been checked; a silent one had not**, which is the whole finding | ✅ **Fixed, asserted twice** | `server-timeout.interceptor.ts` (+ spec, in CI), `app.config.ts`, `scripts/verify-build.sh`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` |
| 26 | **The board's two accessibility defects only exist when there are rooms on it.** `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs` was written in v1.72.0 and reported seven green pages four times; in CI it failed the moment the e2e job's seeded rooms put cards on the board. Both findings were in the room card. **The card's `h3` followed the hero's `h1`** with nothing between — the results region had no heading at all, and the empty state carries an `h2` for exactly this reason while the populated state never got one. The visible count is the heading now. **And the card's `aria-label="View room: {title}"` replaced everything the card shows**: an `aria-label` overrides content, so a screen-reader user tabbing the board heard a list of titles and no rent, no suburb, no availability — every one of which is on the card in text. Removed, so the card is named by its own content and WCAG 2.5.3 holds by construction; the photo's `alt` was the title verbatim two lines above the `h3` that says it, and the avatar's initial stuttered the name beside it. The drive now **fails if the board has no rooms**, because a check that reads the empty state and reports seven green pages is the same as no check. The portal half is closed too, in v1.76.0 — see row 28 | ✅ **Fixed** | `room-card.ts`, `home.ts`, `_spec.scss`, `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs` |
| 27 | **Two more failures were hidden behind the first ones.** A job stops at its first failing step, so the red build had been concealing the steps after it for ten runs. `e2e/locale-and-seo.spec.ts` asserted `/en/pricing` answers **200** — correct when it was written, and the exact soft-404 defect row 20 fixed; it runs in the CI pass that the accessibility step had been skipping, so the assertion outlived the bug it described by four releases. And `withNavigationErrorHandler` read `sessionStorage` unconditionally, so **any** navigation failure during SSR threw `ReferenceError: sessionStorage is not defined` *from inside the handler for that failure* — turning something the renderer could report into something it could not. Found in this repo's own SSR log, not by reading the code. Both fixed | ✅ **Fixed** | `e2e/locale-and-seo.spec.ts`, `app.config.ts` |
| 28 | **Not one screen behind a login had an `h1`.** The portal's section titles are styled divs — 51 of them — so the heading outline of every landlord, tenant and admin page started at the footer and the page itself contributed nothing. Lighthouse cannot see this: all four URLs it audits are public, and every portal screen needs a session. The accessibility drive could not either, and said so in a comment — it covered public pages only because a session was "a different job". So the screens people spend actual time in had never been audited by anything. The drive now registers a landlord and a tenant over the API, signs in through the form using the same helper as the phase drive, and audits **fourteen portal pages** including the admin queues; `PortalShell` renders the `h1` from a `pageTitle` input so a new portal screen cannot ship without one, the 51 divs are `h2`s, and two page-level empty states that followed the `h1` directly are `h2`s as well. It found thirteen failures on the first run and it fails a portal URL that does not answer 200, so a route that quietly stops existing is not a pass | ✅ **Fixed and asserted** | `portal-shell.ts`, 14 page components, `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs`, `scripts/lib/drive-session.mjs` |
| 29 | **/af and /af/pricing were served to crawlers in English, with `lang="af-ZA"` on them.** `provideLocaleRouting` called `void i18n.use(seed)` inside an app initializer — fired and not returned — so nothing waited for the translation bundle before the first render, and `router.url` inside an initializer is `/` whatever was requested, so the seed was English on every page anyway. The real locale arrived later from a `NavigationEnd` subscription, also unawaited. The result was a coin toss: `/zu` came out translated, `/af` and every `/xx/page` did not. Every check pointed away from it — the `lang` attribute was right, the canonical was right, the hreflang set was right, the bundles were complete, and the browser fixed the page a moment after the server got it wrong. | 33 | **Every click on a house ad landed on a JSON 404.** `res.redirect(target)` with a relative target resolves against the origin serving the redirect — which is the API. So the board's sidebar ad, the only call to action a visitor with no rooms to browse actually sees, sent them to `{"message":"Cannot GET /how-it-works","statusCode":404}` at `api.umastande.co.za`, a hostname they have never heard of. Found by clicking it on the live site. The smoke test **had a check for this endpoint** and it passed: it asserted the response was a 301 or 302 and never looked at the `Location` header, so a redirect to the wrong origin was still a redirect. Relative targets now resolve against `frontendUrl`; an advertiser's absolute URL is left alone, because sending a visitor off-site is what a paid click *is*, and a protocol-relative `//evil.com` becomes a path on our own site rather than an open redirect. The smoke test asserts the destination now, and was proven against the bug: `FAIL click redirects to a relative path ("/how-it-works")`. **Separately, production's ad rows are pre-rebrand** — the seed says "New to Mastande?" and the live board says "New to RentBoard?", because `seedHouseAds` upserts with `update: data` and has not been run against production since the rename | ✅ **Fixed and asserted; re-seed production for the branding** | `ads.controller.ts`, `scripts/smoke-test.sh` |

| 35 | **Three legal pages described a product that no longer existed, and nothing looked at prose.** **Terms §4** advertised a subscription table — Pro R349/R2,999, Agency R1,499/R12,999, Renter's Passport R89/R799 — in a binding consumer contract, for four things that cannot be bought: `BILLING_ENABLED` is false, Stripe was dropped in v1.54.0 because it does not operate in South Africa for receiving, PayFast recurring was never built, and the Passport was deliberately made free. | 36 | **The a11y drive reported green while looking at no colour at all.** Row 32 fixed two contrast instances and said the class was still uncovered; this closes it. `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs` runs **axe-core** on every page now, scoped to `color-contrast` deliberately — the full ruleset would land dozens of findings at once and a gate that is red on day one is a gate someone turns off. | 37 | **Every button in the app failed contrast while a pointer was over it, and the check written to catch that passed anyway — twice.** Row 36 parked the pointer to make the resting audit reproducible and, in the same move, removed the only thing that had ever looked at `:hover`. Closing that gap found four more real defects, all from one cause: the global `:where(button):hover` rule paints **every** button terracotta at zero specificity, so any variant without its own hover inherits the background while keeping its resting text colour. The board's `.quick-chip` sat at **1.34:1** (terracotta text on terracotta), the Filters toggle at **2.28:1**, a dashboard button at 2.28:1, and `.btn-sage` at 3.95:1 because sage *lightened* on hover exactly as terracotta had. Setting a background without setting the foreground is the defect; they are one decision, and the rule sets both now. **The check itself was wrong twice, and both are worth recording.** First it dispatched a synthetic `PointerEvent` and read the computed style — CSS `:hover` follows the browser's real pointer, which a dispatched event does not move, so it reported green with the 3.56:1 colour deliberately restored. Driving the real pointer still reported green: `.btn` carries `transition: all .15s`, and `getComputedStyle` returns the value *mid-animation*, so it read the colour the button was leaving. That is also why the original defect was found by accident rather than by design — the pointer had been resting there, long past .15s. It waits 220ms now. Proven by reverting `--terra-deep` and watching it fail with the exact figure, `3.56:1`. A transparent background is resolved to the first painted ancestor rather than skipped, which took the unmeasured share from 44 of 71 controls to a handful | ✅ **Fixed and proven to fail** | `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs`, `styles.scss`, `_spec.scss` |

| 39 | **Nothing in the product had ever asked a landlord what is hard about the job.** Phase 0 builds the survey that decides whether the yard-management tiers are worth building, rather than assuming them. Three tables, an additive migration, and two trigger points: one question right after a landlord marks a room let, and the full set as a skippable card on the dashboard. Question 2 is the contextual one, not the more obvious question 1 — asking "how do you usually find tenants" moments after someone found one through Mastande invites the answer we want to hear. **"Not now" is recorded server-side and honoured for 30 days**, deliberately not in `localStorage`: a dismissal held in a browser means skipping on a phone and being asked again on a laptop an hour later, which is how a prompt becomes something people clear without reading. Eligibility is decided entirely on the server — `audience.minRooms` counts rooms the landlord actually has, and the whole point of targeting "2+ rooms" is to reach people whose situation the product does not know, so a client that decides its own eligibility can be asked to lie about it. Unknown question ids are dropped rather than stored, proven with a planted `evil_injected_key`: four answers in, three recorded. `/admin/surveys` shows counts, never percentages, with the answered-count beside every question, because "60% say rent" means very different things at 5 responses and at 200. **Three bugs caught by running it rather than reading it:** the seed's dry run reported "questions changed" on every run after the first, because Postgres `jsonb` does not preserve object key order and `JSON.stringify` compared the text — the one thing a dry run is for is confirming the stored copy matches, and it said the opposite; `readText()` returned null for bare strings, so questions 6 and 7, the two open questions the survey is really for, would have aggregated as answered by nobody; and the survey card's heading was hardcoded `h3` on my own reasoning that it sat inside a section with an `h2` — it sits directly under the page `h1`, **the identical defect fixed one commit earlier on the admin queues, reintroduced by the person who had just fixed it**, and caught only because the drive walks the rendered page rather than reading templates. `seed-survey.ts` refuses to write when a question id would disappear and responses exist, because answers are keyed by id and would stop resolving — a smaller number that still looks like a result. **Still open: the WhatsApp delivery variant — see row 40** | ✅ **Built and driven; WhatsApp deferred** | `backend/prisma/surveys.ts`, `seed-survey.ts`, `src/modules/surveys/`, `survey-prompt.ts`, `admin/surveys/surveys.ts`, `scripts/smoke-test.sh` |
| 40 | **Every outbound WhatsApp message is free-form text, and the sign-in code is one of them.** Found while designing Phase 0's WhatsApp survey variant, and it is the larger finding. Meta permits `type: 'text'` only inside the **24-hour customer service window** — within 24 hours of the user messaging the business. A business-initiated message outside that window is rejected (error 131047) unless it uses a **pre-approved template**. This codebase has no template anywhere: `grep` for `type: 'template'` returns nothing, and `.env.example` has `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, `_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `_VERIFY_TOKEN` and `_APP_SECRET` with no slot for a template name. Two consequences. **`sendOtp` will not deliver**: it sends the sign-in code as plain text, so for anyone who has not messaged the business in the previous 24 hours — essentially every sign-in — Meta rejects it. It looks fine today only because no credentials are configured, so it logs the code as a warning in development and never calls Meta. That is Phase 7g groundwork that fails the moment it is switched on. **And a survey blast cannot work as specified**, since reaching a landlord who does not open the portal is business-initiated by definition. The variant is deferred to Phase 7g rather than built against an assumption that does not hold, because the template work is shared between the survey and the OTP and is better done once. **Requires a Meta console step that cannot be done from this repo**: submit and get approval for an authentication template for the OTP and a utility/marketing template for survey outreach, then add the template names to `.env.example`. Nothing here is fixed — this row is the record that it is known | ⚠️ **Half closed in v1.114.0 (Phase 8i)** — `sendOtp` now sends an AUTHENTICATION template when `WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_OTP` is set, the body shape is driven against a stub rather than against documentation, and credentials with no template warn at boot naming 131047. | 41 | **Phase 1 was already built; what the audit found instead was three dead tables describing products that do not exist.** Every item the Phase 1 brief asks for is in place and driven: the landlord verification UI and admin queue, the Renter's Passport flow, the two-sided badge, the audit trail, the post-tenancy flag, and POPIA retention. | 42 | **Phase 2 Tier 1: the fields the brief said were already in the schema did not exist, and adding the room page to the accessibility drive found a live WCAG failure.** The brief asks to "surface the property-level split that already exists — `propertyAmenities`, `houseRules`, `currentHousemates`, `housemateProfile` on `Property`". None of the four existed anywhere; `Property` had name, suburb, city, province and its rooms, and everything shared-living sat on `Room`, per room. So they are **added**, not surfaced — a migration, not a display task. They belong to the house because four rooms at one address have one kitchen, one set of rules and one group of housemates, and held per room a landlord types them four times and the copies drift in front of tenants. Named `sharedAmenities` rather than `propertyAmenities` so it can never read as a synonym for `Room.amenities`. `HousemateProfile` defaults to **`unstated`, a distinct value from `mixed`**: `mixed` is a claim about who someone would be living with, silence is not, and defaulting to a claim nobody made would put a description in front of a tenant that no landlord wrote — `unstated` is never rendered anywhere. **This also unblocks Phase 6**, which asks to surface `housemateProfile` for sublet listings and would have had nothing to read. **Bulk relist** calls `RoomsService.relist` per room rather than a faster `updateMany`: relisting archives the previous cycle's applications, increments `relistCount` so the applicant list matches the cycle, resets counters, clears `letAt` and notifies matching tenants, and a bulk copy would be the copy that misses the next fix. It **skips** what it cannot relist and names why per room, because four let and two listed means "put the four back" and refusing the batch would make the button useless in exactly the case it is for. **The drive had never audited a room page** — a room's URL carries its id and the list is literal paths — so the most-read page after the board had never been checked for heading order or colour. It follows the first card on the board now, and the first run failed on `H4 after H2 ("Before you pay a deposit")`: the ad slot hardcoded `<h4>` for a house advert's headline regardless of placement, a live WCAG 1.3.1 failure, fixed the same way as the survey card — `headingLevel` is the host's to declare, because a component cannot know its own depth. **Two of the three named bulk actions could not be built**: boost does not exist (row 41) and house rules were not a field until this row. **Tier 2 is deliberately not started** — the brief gates it on survey data, and no survey responses exist yet | ✅ **Tier 1 complete; Tier 2 correctly gated** | `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `properties.service.ts`, `rooms.service.ts`, `yard.ts`, `room-detail.ts`, `ad-slot.ts`, `scripts/a11y-drive.mjs` |
| 43 | **Phase 4b, expenses: the money screen was half-built and reading as whole.** Rent tracking already said what came in, so the yard answered "how am I doing" with one number and no way to see it was one-sided — half an answer about money is worse than none, because the reader does not know it is half. An `Expense` hangs off the **property**, not the room: one municipal bill, one plumber, one gate motor for the whole address, and forcing every cost onto a room would make the common case a lie. `roomId` narrows it when a cost genuinely is one room's and is `SetNull`, not `Cascade` — **deleting a listing must not erase what the landlord spent on it**, which they still need at tax time. The money card carries a **basis line** stating which rent months are counted, because a figure whose rules are invisible is one someone plans around and is wrong about, and rent on rooms in no yard is reported separately so the per-yard rows need not silently fail to sum. Amounts render through a new `zarCents: 'exact'` mode — two decimals; the rounded default is right for a listing price and wrong for a reconciliation. **Nothing stores `paidBy` or `sharePercent`**: Phase 6 wants co-tenants splitting a bill and the way not to block that is to keep an expense a record of a *cost*, letting any future split be its own table pointing here rather than guessing now at semantics nobody has specified. **POPIA:** a receipt is the landlord's own business record, not information about a tenant, so unlike a verification document it is **kept** rather than deleted on a decision; no tenant is named on an expense and it is never shown to anyone but its owner | ✅ **Built and driven** | `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `src/modules/properties/expenses.service.ts`, `yard.ts`, `zar-cents.pipe.ts` |

| 45 | **Phase 4d, the contractor directory: the design decision is what it refuses to do.** Five trades, and **no landlord-facing way to add a provider**. An open directory of tradespeople is a directory of whoever registered fastest, and recommending a stranger to someone's tenants is a reputational risk this platform would carry with no way to manage it — an admin adds a name or it is not there. **No booking and no payment:** the product's job ends at "here is the number", `tel:` to call and `wa.me` to message, and the landlord and the plumber arrange it between themselves. | 47 | 🔴 **Nothing in this codebase had ever deleted a stored file, while three live statements — one statutory — said uploaded documents were deleted.** Found while building Phase 4e, and fixed before it. Deciding a verification cleared `documentPath`, set a column called `documentDeletedAt`, and wrote an audit event reading *"The uploaded document was deleted. Only this outcome is kept — POPIA s.26."* There is no ImageKit SDK in `package.json` and `grep` for a delete call returned **nothing**: the row stopped pointing at the file and the file stayed, indefinitely. What said otherwise: the **privacy policy**'s ImageKit row ("deleted once reviewed — only the outcome is kept"), the **PAIA manual** ("Documents are deleted on decision") — a statutory document — and the line shown to a tenant **at the moment they upload their ID** ("deleted as soon as someone has looked at it"). The person relying on those is the one who handed over their identity document. **Why it survived:** the single smoke assertion covering the promise read `documentDeletedAt != null`, which is us asserting our own timestamp and is true whether or not anything was deleted — and it sat behind `ADMIN_TOKEN`, so it had **never run**. A skipped check reads like a passing one at a glance. Compounding it, the admin queue linked "Open document ↗" to the bare stored path, which resolved against the app's own origin and 404'd, so **every private upload was write-only** and no reviewer saw a document either way. **The fix:** `FileDeletion`, a durable queue whose row is written in the SAME transaction as the change that orphans the file, so a committed decision always has a pending deletion behind it and a rolled-back one deletes nothing; `deletedAt` set from ImageKit's response and **never optimistically**, with retries, an attempt cap, and hourly draining that logs stuck rows at error level; `documentDeletedAt` renamed **`documentWithdrawnAt`** (hand-written `RENAME COLUMN`, not Prisma's generated DROP+ADD, which would have discarded the timestamp on every decided request — tested on a scratch database, the value survives exactly); the audit trail split into `document_withdrawn` when it leaves view and `document_deleted` only once storage confirms; and `GET /admin/storage/status` so the promise is **checkable** — outstanding, stuck, and the age of the oldest waiting file, with no path in the payload. **How it is verified:** `scripts/storage-drive.mjs` IS an ImageKit stub, so the assertion is that a DELETE arrived for the right file rather than that the code contains a `fetch` — and with the stub returning 500 it checks the queue reports a failure, does NOT stamp the row deleted, and that a retry completes it. Reverting the fix makes three assertions fail and the script exit 1; checked, not assumed. ⚠️ **One thing this cannot settle:** whether ImageKit ACCEPTS our signed URLs and delete calls. The algorithm matches their SDK and the drive recomputes the signature independently, but there are no credentials here — **confirm on staging with a real key before launch** | ✅ **Fixed and driven; needs one check against a real ImageKit key** | `backend/src/modules/storage/`, `verification.service.ts`, `prisma/migrations/20260929023500_rename_document_deleted_to_withdrawn/`, `scripts/storage-drive.mjs` |

| 55 | **Phase 7g, part one: email is optional now, and making it so surfaced 22 places that assumed it was not.** The decision was taken as recommended — email optional, phone an alternative login identifier, at least one required. **"At least one" is a database CHECK constraint**, not an application rule: an account with neither is an account nobody can sign into or notify, and that is too important to leave to whichever code path runs. Added `NOT VALID` then `VALIDATE` so the exclusive lock covers only the catalogue change. ⚠️ **Phone uniqueness is a PARTIAL index on verified numbers only**, and that was forced by the data: the column has never been unique, and the dev database had **forty accounts sharing one number** — production may have people who mistyped or a household sharing a handset. A full unique index would fail to create, and the only way to force it would be nulling other people's saved numbers, which is destroying user data to satisfy a constraint. Verified numbers are the ones that decide a login (`requestCode` only ever finds `phoneVerified: true`), so unverified duplicates cannot make sign-in ambiguous. Prisma cannot express a partial unique index and will offer to drop it on `migrate dev` — do not let it; this repo applies hand-written migrations with `migrate deploy`. All three safeguards were tested by trying to violate them: an account with neither contact is refused, a second verified account on one number is refused, an unverified duplicate is allowed. **The 22 call sites are the real work.** Every notification was `sendSomethingEmail(user.email, …)`, so a phone-only landlord would have silently missed their applications — the worst consequence of the change. Rather than 16 copies of the same channel decision (and a 17th notification that would not have it), one `NoticeRouter` decides: when there is an address the caller's own email function runs **unchanged**, and otherwise a `Notice` row is written. ⚠️ **WhatsApp is not the fallback and cannot be:** every outbound message here is free-form text, which Meta permits only inside the 24-hour window, so a WhatsApp-only fallback fails for exactly the people who have not messaged recently. The notice is written **first and unconditionally**; WhatsApp is best-effort on top, with `whatsappSentAt` set only from a successful send and the refusal reason kept — the same rule as `FileDeletion`, because this codebase has already shipped a column whose name asserted a delivery that never happened. Judgement calls, not blanket patches: a password **reset** is skipped with no address (their route back is the code) while "your password was changed" goes through the router, because it is a security alert; a magic **link** needs an address by definition; and 🔴 **PayFast requires `email_address`, so a phone-only landlord is refused at checkout** with a plain instruction — a placeholder address means the receipt goes nowhere and a disputed payment has no paper trail. See `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §11 | ✅ **Built and driven; one product decision open** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20260929090000_phone_login_identity/`, `notifications/notice-router.service.ts`, 9 services, `scripts/otp-drive.mjs` |
| 56 | **Phase 7g, part two: `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5` had been sitting in the OTP service counting nothing, and the lookup shape was why.** The service's own header admitted the constant was read by nothing. The reason was not a missed wiring: `verifyCode` did `findUnique({ tokenHash: hash(submittedCode) })`, so a wrong code matched no row — **and you cannot increment a counter on a token you could not find.** The lookup is now by whose code it is, then a comparison, so there is something to count against. Four hardening changes, all previously absent: **attempts are counted per account** and the code burns at five (the `@Throttle` on the route was believed to be a per-IP backstop under it; ⚠️ **it was not — see row 58, no rate limit was in force anywhere in the API**, so for that whole period a constant nothing read was the only thing between a six-digit code and an attacker); **one live code at a time**, since issuing a second without spending the first left up to three valid codes in flight and tripled the guessing surface for no benefit; a **daily cap** of ten, because the 15-minute limit stops a burst but not 288 messages a day to a number you do not own; and the stored hash is now an **HMAC keyed with the server secret** rather than a bare `sha256(phone:code)` — six digits is a million candidates, so anyone who could read `auth_tokens` recovered every outstanding code in about a second, and a keyed MAC makes a read-only database leak (a backup, a replica, a dashboard) stop handing out live sign-in codes. Comparison is `timingSafeEqual`. Also fixed: the success log printed `user.email`, which is `undefined` for exactly the phone-only accounts this feature is for. ⚠️ **I could not complete the negative test through HTTP** — disabling the limit and retrying stalled rather than failing cleanly, which I attributed to the per-IP throttle. Row 58 shows there was no throttle to trip, so that attribution was wrong; the stall had some other cause and the falsification below is what the claim rests on. Falsified directly instead: setting `attempts` past the cap and calling verify once returns "Too many wrong codes", proving the guard reads the column. The drive also had to be made idempotent twice — two hardcoded phone numbers meant the partial unique index correctly refused the second run, and the index doing its job read as a broken drive | ✅ **Hardened and driven** | `backend/src/modules/auth/phone-otp.service.ts`, `scripts/otp-drive.mjs` |
| 57 | **Phase 7g, part three: a person with only a phone number can now create their own account — and nobody can create one for them.** Signing in by phone shipped first, which left an odd gap: a landlord could sign in with WhatsApp only if somebody had already made them an account with an email address, so the WhatsApp-first people the feature exists for could not get through the front door. | 58 | 🔴 **Nothing in this API was rate limited. Nineteen `@Throttle` decorators across eight controllers had never done anything, and `/auth/login` had no decorator at all.** Two independent faults, and the second hid the first: `ThrottlerGuard` was never registered — not as an `APP_GUARD`, not on a controller, not on a route — and the root throttler was named `global` while every decorator keys `default`, a key matching no configured throttler being silently ignored. So password guessing against `/auth/login` was unmetered, and every comment in the codebase asserting that "the per-route `@Throttle` is what actually limits code guessing" was false (two of them are corrected in place; see row 56). **Found by probing, not reading:** eight requests to a route marked `limit: 5` all returned `200`. The decorator looked right in review, which is the whole problem — this is the same class of defect as a `MAX_ATTEMPTS` nothing reads and a `documentDeletedAt` nothing deletes, and it is the third one in three releases. Fixed by pairing a `ThrottlerGuard` with every `@Throttle` **per route rather than globally**, deliberately: the SSR server calls this API for every server-rendered page from one address, so a global per-IP guard would throttle the whole site under load. `scripts/throttle-lint.mjs` fails the build if a `@Throttle` is ever added without its guard or the names drift apart again, and both the smoke suite and the sign-up drive now prove a real `429` **and** that the window refills — a limiter that never refills is a lockout. **The numbers moved, and that is a product decision worth seeing:** mobile carriers here put very large numbers of subscribers behind one address, so limits of 5 and 10 per 15 minutes would lock out a township sharing an IP; auth is now 15/30, `/auth/login` 30 per 15 minutes, register 60 per hour (ten an hour would refuse the eleventh landlord in the queue at a community sign-up drive, which is a growth channel rather than an attack). Public **scam reporting** went 5 → 20 per hour on the same reasoning plus an asymmetry: a false report costs an admin a minute, a suppressed one leaves a scam listing up. ⚠️ **Still missing and recorded in `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §12:** there is no per-ACCOUNT login lockout, so a slow distributed spray at one known email is still possible; and the counters are in memory per instance, so scaling past one instance roughly doubles every limit | ✅ **Fixed, linted and proven** | `backend/src/app.module.ts`, 8 controllers, `scripts/throttle-lint.mjs`, `scripts/verify-build.sh` |
| 59 | 🔴 **The in-app notice channel was write-only. `NoticeRouter` had been writing rows since v1.85.0 and nothing could read them.** Its own comment calls the notice "the channel that cannot fail for reasons outside our control" — true of the write, and meaningless without a read. So a phone-only landlord's "you have a new applicant" was recorded faithfully, refused by WhatsApp outside Meta's 24-hour window, and shown to nobody: a notification system that was complete except for the part where somebody is notified. It was on the outstanding list as a screen to build; it is a hole in the fallback, and the moment people could create phone-only accounts through the front door (row 57) it became the hole those accounts fall into. Fixed in the same release. `GET /notices`, `GET /notices/unread` (count **and** list, so a nav badge costs one call rather than two), `PATCH /notices/:id/read`, `POST /notices/read-all` — each scoped to the caller **in the WHERE clause**, so another person's notice is a miss and not a refusal: a 403 would confirm the notice exists. `/account/notices` serves both roles with the sidebar of whichever role you are, and both dashboards carry the unread badge, because a screen nobody can find is the same as no screen. Because Prisma's `String @id` is TEXT and not `uuid`, Postgres accepted the malformed value and matched nothing, so **`otp-drive.mjs` had been leaving its throwaway account behind on every run while reporting that it cleaned up.** It surfaced only because a route with a `ParseUUIDPipe` was handed the same string and said so. | 64 | 🔴 **The dashboard audit the brief asked for found three things Phase 7b had broken, none of which could error.** The ask was to lead both dashboards with "what needs my attention right now", confirm the plain-English principle is **in the real components and not just the design doc**, and put persistent "Add a room"/"Add a property" entry points on the landlord home. New `room_view_days`: one row per room per day, a counter rather than a row per view, **no viewer identity** (POPIA s.10 — the figure is "how many times"; who looked is not needed), the owner's own look not counted. | 67 | 🔴 **Item 3: pausing an account and ending one — and `DELETE FROM users` would have deleted other people's records.** The foreign keys were read out of postgres before anything was designed: a user row cascades into their rooms (and so into other tenants' applications, saved rooms and reviews), into applications (and so into **messages** — both sides of every conversation), into tenancies (and so into `rent_periods`, the tenant's own proof of payment), and into reviews they wrote about somebody else. A landlord closing their account would have erased their tenant's application, the tenant's rent history and the landlord's half of their conversation. **POPIA s.24 is a right to have YOUR personal information deleted** — not somebody else's, and not a right to destroy a record two parties share. So the mechanism is a **tombstone**: the row stays, the person goes. Name becomes `Former member`, email becomes a reserved `.invalid` address that can never reach anybody, password hash, phone, photo, verification outcomes, notices and sessions all go, and the avatar leaves through the existing `file_deletions` queue rather than merely being unlinked — all in one `$transaction`, so the queue row commits with the erasure. ⚠️ **Pausing deliberately does NOT reuse `isActive`.** That flag is the admin suspension, refused on every sign-in path with "contact support"; reusing it would have looked like one line of work and shipped an account nobody could ever reopen — so `deactivatedAt` is separate and signing in still works, because that is the only way back. Which forced the other half: since `isActive` stays **true** while paused, every existing `user: { isActive: true }` filter would have kept a paused landlord's public page lit, so one shared `PUBLIC_USER`/`SIGN_IN_USER` rule now covers the storefront, the sitemap, the OTP paths and the listing bot. Waking up does **not** republish the rooms — one may have been let while the account slept, and advertising a taken room to people who then apply for it is worse than making the landlord press publish — so it reports how many are waiting instead of omitting them silently. The screen is its own route reached by a **link** from settings, because an irreversible control should not be one scroll below "change your name", and it lists what STAYS with the reason **before** anything is typed, counted from the person's own rows. It takes three things to close: the password, the typed word `DELETE`, and a box that arrives **unticked** and is refused when ABSENT rather than read as false. Ten attempts per fifteen minutes, not five, because the form's four honest refusals plus one correct attempt is exactly five and a limit that stops the owner before it stops an attacker is the wrong limit. 🔴 **The defect this phase found was not on this screen.** The step-up checks answered **401**, and the frontend reads a 401 on any non-auth endpoint as an expired token: refresh silently, retry once — which re-sent the same wrong password, got 401 again, and on a second failure said *"Your session has expired"*, cleared the session and went to the login page. **So typing your own password wrong logged you out**, and it had been true of `/account/settings` since that screen shipped: both its forms carry an inline `.field-error` written for a message that could never render. Observed in a browser, not inferred. They are **403** now — the request was authenticated; the password typed into the form is what was refused — with an `INLINE_ERRORS` context token so a form showing its own message does not also get a modal. ⚠️ **The smoke suite had a check here and it could not fail:** one line asserted 401 for a wrong current password and another asserted 401 for no auth at all, so the suite could not tell the two apart any better than the browser could. 🔴 **And a 34px button on a screen built this phase** — "Pause my account" and "Use my account again" measured 34px at all four widths, under WCAG 2.5.8, because the base `.btn` sets padding and `line-height: 1` and no `min-height`; fixed here, measured across the public pages (13 of 23 visible buttons, as low as 32px) and recorded in Outstanding §13 rather than changed globally inside a phase about closing accounts. ⚠️ **How that was found is the part worth keeping: the previous version of my own drive computed the smallest control height, the pause button included, and never asserted it.** The number was measured and thrown away — same family as a `MAX_ATTEMPTS` nothing reads. ⚠️ **Three more faults in my own tooling, each of which reported success:** the drive read the screen before the async preview existed, and because `locator.count()` does not auto-wait while `innerText()` does, two checks disagreed about the same screen and one invented a defect; it **crashed** on the missing form instead of failing, so the three sections after it — including the one covering the settings screen — never ran at all, the fourth time that has happened here; and a hand-rolled `jq` assertion reported a 429 as a product defect, twice in opposite directions ("own code did not validate" AND "unknown code validated"), so the suite now has a `throttled` helper for assertions that read a body rather than a status. ⚠️ **`saved_rooms` is a table nothing writes to** — read in two places, deleted from in one, no create anywhere; saved rooms are entirely localStorage, and the service's own comment said otherwise. The first drive POSTed to a save route that does not exist, so a survival check passed against a row that had never been created. | 68 | 🔴 **Items 18-22 and 25: six "styling" items, and a class that had never had any effect.** The wizard's Back, Cancel and Next measured **three identical solid terra buttons, 33px tall, 6px apart** at 360px — one of which abandons the form. Cancel carried `class="btn btn-ghost-light"`, a real global class written to make it a bordered secondary button, and it had done nothing since the day it was typed: a component's own bare `button { background: var(--terra) }` outranks any single global class, because Angular's emulated encapsulation appends an attribute selector. The wizard's "Next" is disabled until the description reaches 50 characters, so that is a button somebody taps while nothing happens. Found by hiding each child of the step in turn: a **fieldset** will not shrink below its min-content (a quirk no other element has), and **`main.portal-main` is a grid item**, so its `min-width: auto` is its min-content and a grid TRACK is sized by its widest item — one screen wanting 354px made the track 386px and stretched the nav to match. Both needed `min-width: 0`; either alone left the other able to widen the page, and without the second every portal screen is one wide element away from the same bug with the nav blamed again. 🔴 **Item 25: the applicant action row measured 25-27px tall and 8px apart** — Accept beside Reject, on the landlord's most consequential screen (44px and 10px now; a size fix rather than a redesign, because the decision is reversible for 30 minutes and the card says so). Its media query was `max-width: 400px`, which is not one of this codebase's three breakpoints, so at 430px — an iPhone 14 Pro Max — the mobile layout did not apply at all. 🔴 **And the applicant card could only be opened with a pointer**: the header was a `<div>` with a click handler and no role, tabindex or key handler, so a landlord on a keyboard could reach no applicant's details, references, Accept or Reject. Now a `role="status"` line where the form was, **naming the property** (the screen lists several), held until the next edit rather than cleared on a timer; the drive asserts the message, its role, the name **and** that the rules really are on the row, because a confirmation alone does not prove a save. 🔴 **Item 22: the money-spent section had no stylesheet.** Not a thin one — `.yard-shared-form`, `.yard-shared-form__actions`, `.yard-expenses`, `.expense-list`, `.expense`, `.expense__main`, `.expense-form` and `.expense-form__actions` had **no rules anywhere in the product**. | 69 | 🔴 **"Admin Cannot Delete User Accounts" was true, and §5.25 had already recorded why it mattered.** A phone-only account has no password, so `/account/close` cannot confirm it; that screen tells the person to ask us and **there was nothing behind the asking**. POPIA s.24 is a right, not a feature request — if the only self-service path needs a credential some accounts do not have, the operator has to be able to act. What the admin surface had was `PATCH /admin/users/:id/active`, which suspends, and nothing else. Now `DELETE /admin/users/:id`, **separate from the suspend route on purpose**: suspension is reversible, keeps the email and is a moderation decision we make, while closure is irreversible, erases the email and is the owner's decision we carry out — one endpoint doing both with a flag is how somebody suspends an account and ends it. ⚠️ **The erasure is the SAME code**, extracted to one private method both paths call. Two erasures that start identical drift, and the one that drifts is the one nobody drives, so the day a column is added to the self-service path the admin path would quietly stop erasing it. The preview is **byte-for-byte** the owner's, asserted as a string comparison: an admin acting on a request should read what that person would have read, and a separate admin-flavoured summary is a second thing to keep true. It takes a written request of at least ten characters, the word CLOSE, and a box that arrives **unticked** and is refused when ABSENT — because after this runs the email, name and number are gone, so **the audit row is the only lasting evidence it was asked for** and "ok" is not evidence. It lives on the detail screen rather than in the dashboard's user list where suspension is: suspension belongs inline, this needs room to show what it will do first. **The new `account_closures` table holds no identity at all** — no email, no name, no phone, no IP — because an audit trail that keeps what the erasure removed defeats the erasure it audits; the user id suffices since the tombstone survives and resolves to "Former member". The drive asserts the column list **by name**, this being the kind of table somebody helpfully adds a `userEmail` to later. 🔴 **And the suspension toggle could have resurrected a closed account**: `setUserActive(id, true)` on a tombstone had nothing stopping it, so it would set `isActive: true` on a row whose name, email and phone are gone. Sign-in still refuses it — `deletedAt` is checked everywhere, which is exactly why the erasure sets `isActive: false` as a second independent reason — but **the admin screen would have shown the account as active and the operator would have believed it**. A control reporting a state the system does not have is the defect this codebase keeps shipping. ⚠️ **Falsification rescued a guard I nearly called redundant.** An admin cannot close their own account here; removed, the request still fails because the ADMIN-role check catches it — but it fails saying *"use the database directly"*, which tells an admin to go DELETE their own user row: the one action the whole tombstone design exists to prevent, because it cascades into other people's applications, conversations and tenancies. ⚠️ **Both drives promote their own admin with one SQL statement**, because every other admin drive here reads `ADMIN_EMAIL` and **skips** without it — which is how ninety-two smoke checks had never run once. The smoke guards for this route are unconditional too: the guards on the most destructive route in the product should not be among the checks that only run when somebody remembers an env var. ⚠️ **And the admin screens had never been accessibility-audited** (§5.26 recorded it as a gap). Running the a11y drive with an admin takes it from 17 portal pages to **27**, including `/admin/users/:id` — the only admin screen with a destructive form, so exactly where a red-on-pink panel fails contrast while looking deliberate. | 70 | 🔴 **The "Who to Call" page already claimed verification. Nothing in the product recorded a check of any kind.** `/landlord/services` opened with *"People we have checked out and can pass on"* and its empty state read *"It is names we have checked, not an open directory"*. `ServiceProvider` had category, name, phone, whatsapp, areas, note, active and an unused `sponsoredUntil` — **no column, no table, no outcome, nothing**. The admin supplies the date rather than the server stamping `now()`, because a call made on Tuesday and recorded on Thursday is a Tuesday check. The screen lists **which** checks exist, so one check and three checks no longer read alike — which is exactly what the blanket claim did for both. ⚠️ **`active` now requires the phone check, enforced in the service AND as a CHECK constraint**, because the rule matters more than the route: a seed script, a direct UPDATE or a second admin surface must not be able to publish an unchecked name. And it is evaluated against what the row will **be**, not against the request — `{active:true}` with no phone field is the common case (ticking "list this person" on an already-checked row), while `{active:true, phoneConfirmedAt:null}` would otherwise clear the check and publish in one call. **Only outcomes persist**: `idCheckedAt` records that a document was *seen* and there is deliberately no `documentPath`, the rule `VerificationRequest` already follows (POPIA s.19). The drive asserts the column list **by name**, because "store the ID so we can re-check it" is a reasonable-sounding thing somebody adds later. Rather than patch them to pass, both now assert the rule themselves, and the UI drive records the check through the admin screen's own "Record it" button rather than over the API, because the button is what a person uses. ⚠️ **The directory had no smoke coverage at all** — not one check on the endpoints behind that screen. Seven now, and the ones that matter need no admin token. ⚠️ **And a fault in my own new smoke section that reported success**: "every listed provider has had their number rung" sat four requests below its subject, so it read the 401 body from an unauthenticated POST, and its own `else true` guard passed it.
