| 71 | 💰 **Contractor lead fees: the record is built and nothing collects, because inspecting the models said it could not.** The brief said to inspect the contractor, lead and user models before implementing payments and to identify where pricing should be configurable. Inspecting them answered the first question outright: **a contractor is not a user** — `ServiceProvider` has no `userId` and no email, just a name, a number and the areas they cover, so they cannot sign in, see a bill, accept terms or dispute a charge — and **there was no lead model at all**, so nothing recorded that a number had been passed on either. PayFast exists for a once-off landlord verification fee; billing an accumulating balance to somebody with no account is a different arrangement entirely. So the honest scope is the RECORD — what was passed on, to whom, on what day, and what it comes to at a rate somebody agreed — and invoicing happens outside the product, by a person. **Collecting would need contractor accounts first**: a portal, terms acceptance, a bill they can read and a way to disagree with it, which is a product and the owner's decision (Outstanding §16). The drives assert the stop: no `paid`/`invoiced`/`settled` column on a lead, and no `pay`/`invoice`/`checkout`/`charge` route under the services module, so adding one fails a check whose comment says why it exists. ⚠️ This does NOT touch free-to-list/free-to-apply — the money would come from a contractor receiving leads, a third party, never from a landlord listing a room or a tenant applying. **There is no price anywhere in the code**: not a constant, not a default, not an env var, not an example in the API docs, not a placeholder in the form. `contractor_lead_rates` ships empty, and with no rate set a lead is recorded with no fee and not billable — the product counts what it sent and declines to put a figure on it, which the admin screen says in words rather than showing R0.00. Rates are per category, effective-dated and **insert-only**, because leads point at the rate they were created under: editing would re-price history and deleting would leave a lead claiming a figure from nowhere. The fee is **snapshotted**, so tripling the price next month does not re-price what was already sent and a rate dated next month is not in force yet; `billable` is **stored** rather than derived, so agreeing terms in November cannot make October billable. Nothing is billable without **agreement AND a rate** — charging somebody for leads they never agreed to receive is not defensible, and since they cannot agree in-product an admin records how they did, in at least ten characters. One lead per landlord per **day**, by a unique index rather than read-then-write (two taps in the same second would both see nothing and both insert), and only a **listed** provider generates one. Priced and counted-only are reported **separately** because "we sent you eleven and are charging for four" is the honest sentence. The disclaimer lives in the **payload**, not just the screen, so an export cannot drift from it — the UI drive asserts the two are byte-identical. **The landlord is told and never charged**: the page says pressing Call records the hand-over, that *you are never charged*, who we would ask instead, and that we do not tell the tradesperson which landlord it was (POPIA s.18). The handler is fire-and-forget so the dialler opens whether or not the write succeeds — a failed bookkeeping request must never stand between somebody with a burst pipe and a plumber, and the drive checks the `tel:` link is untouched. ⚠️ **The landlord's identity is kept, and the migration says why**: dedupe needs same-landlord-or-different, and a contractor disputing "you billed me for twelve leads" can only be answered from rows that tell twelve landlords apart from one landlord twelve times. It is never disclosed to the contractor and **is nulled when the landlord closes their account** — the lead is a third party's record and stays, the person does not — asserted in the account-lifecycle drive in BOTH halves, because a check that only asserted the row survived would pass with the person still named in it. ⚠️ **Three faults in my own drives, each reporting something false:** the drive was **not idempotent** (rates are insert-only by design, so the second run read back the rate the first had set and reported the wrong fee — a drive that only passes on a clean database gets "fixed" by weakening it); the UI drive **clicked the first provider on the page** while counting leads for its own fixture, reporting "pressing Call took the count from 0 to 0" about a product that was recording correctly; and it **cleared only the plumber rates** while asserting a panel that is a statement about the whole table, so another drive's locksmith rates kept it hidden and the check called the screen silent about a state the fixture never created | 💰 **Recorded, not collected — awaiting your decision; 33 + 43 drive checks, 472 smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20261004190000_contractor_leads/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/services/{contractor-leads.service.ts,services.controller.ts,services.module.ts,dto/contractor-lead.dto.ts}`, `modules/account/account-lifecycle.service.ts`, `frontend/.../core/services/service-directory.service.ts`, `features/landlord/services/services.ts`, `features/admin/services/services.ts`, `scripts/contractor-leads-drive.mjs`, `scripts/contractor-leads-ui-drive.mjs`, `scripts/{account-lifecycle-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.29, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §16 |
| 72 | **Item 26: inviting an applicant to a viewing — and there was no concept of a room viewing anywhere in the product.** `ApplicationStatus` runs pending → viewed → shortlisted → accepted, and `viewed` means **the landlord opened the application** — §5.1's own table says so. So "I'll meet you Saturday at four" lived in the message thread and nowhere else: no date either side could look up, nothing the tenant could answer yes or no to, and nothing to say whether it had been agreed. 🔴 **The decision that matters is the address.** The board shows a suburb and not a street on purpose; `Property.addressLine` does hold one, and the form where a landlord types it promises, in these words, *"Only you see this. It is never on a listing and never sent to an applicant."* So the meeting place **cannot** be pre-filled from it — doing so would break a promise the product made in writing, on the form where the landlord typed it, and they would never know. There is no `useMyPropertyAddress` flag, no fallback, no suggestion: the landlord types where to meet for this viewing for this person, and the form says **in bold** that it is sent to them and that their saved address stays private. It is the one moment an address is deliberately disclosed, to one named applicant, chosen deliberately. Hung off the **application**, because it already ties one tenant to one room and carries the letting cycle — separate room and tenant columns would allow a viewing for somebody who never applied, which is a stranger handed a residential address. Refused on a **closed** application (rejected, withdrawn or archived): sending somebody who has been told no a time and an address is a mistake. **One open invitation at a time**, because two times to turn up at is none. **Only the tenant answers** — a landlord accepting on their behalf turns a proposal into an appointment nobody agreed to — and **either side cancels**, because a landlord whose geyser burst and a tenant who cannot get transport are the same situation from opposite ends. Cancelling **clears the answer**, since "they accepted" stops being true of a viewing that is not happening and a row claiming both contradicts itself. Three CHECK constraints hold the blank place, the undated answer and the undated cancellation at the database. `Africa/Johannesburg` is named explicitly, because the server runs UTC and a viewing described two hours early is somebody standing at a gate alone. **The safety line travels with the invitation**: this product's own report reasons include `upfront_payment_demanded`, so the risk is already known to the codebase and a viewing is exactly when it happens — the warning is in the notice at the moment of invitation and on the panel where somebody looks the night before. The tenant's panel is its own section rather than a task-inbox row because it is the only thing on that dashboard with a **place and a time**, and burying "Saturday 16:00, the blue gate in Tembisa" among "three applications awaiting reply" is how somebody misses it. It renders nothing when there is nothing. ⚠️ **Falsifying the address check taught something about the check.** The first attempt made the service fall back to `addressLine` on a blank meeting place — and the check stayed **GREEN**, because the DTO's `@MinLength(3)` refuses a blank before the service runs, so the fallback could not fire. A leak arrives as somebody helpfully appending "The address is …" to the notice, which it does catch — proven by doing it. Hence it searches the stored row, the tenant's notices AND the whole payload the API hands the tenant; only one of the three would have caught the realistic version. ⚠️ **`nav-audit.mjs` caught a dead link I had just written** — the safety line pointed at `/report`, which does not exist (reporting is a dialog on the room page). That gate earned its keep inside an hour. ⚠️ **And the a11y drive was about to audit the new panel EMPTY**: it renders nothing when there is nothing, so its seeded tenant had no invitation and the one panel on that screen with a red-tinted safety box and a sage "you are coming" line — exactly where contrast fails while looking deliberate — would never have been measured. The same mistake Phase 7c made across every portal screen; the seed creates a viewing now, and it passes | ✅ **Built, driven and falsified; 35 + 41 drive checks, 478 smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20261004200000_room_viewings/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/applications/{viewings.service.ts,applications.controller.ts,applications.module.ts,dto/viewing.dto.ts}`, `frontend/.../core/services/applications.service.ts`, `features/landlord/applicants/applicants.ts`, `shared/components/viewing-invitations/`, `features/tenant/dashboard/tenant-dashboard.ts`, `scripts/viewings-drive.mjs`, `scripts/viewings-ui-drive.mjs`, `scripts/{a11y-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.30 |
| 73 | 🔴 **`sponsoredUntil` was a writable control with no reader — and it was in every landlord's browser.** Four comments said nothing reads it (schema, ordering method, DTO, frontend model) and all four were true; the ordering method even explains why it stays out of the `ORDER BY`, and a drive asserts sponsoring does not move a provider up the list. **What no comment said is that it was writable.** `PATCH /api/services/admin/:id {"sponsoredUntil":"2027-12-31"}` returned **HTTP 200**, echoed the date and stored it — and `"1999-01-01"` was accepted too, the only rule on it being `@IsISO8601()`. So an admin could sell a placement, record it, be told it worked, and have nothing happen: the house defect with money attached, where the payment would be real and the placement would not exist. ⚠️ **The subtler half: it was in the `ordered()` SELECT**, which is this codebase's deliberate allowlist — the mechanism that keeps `checkedByAdminId` from landlords — so every landlord received it while nothing in `frontend/src/app` read it. The decision not to sell placement was held by a comment in a backend method while the data to defeat it sat in the browser, typed in the model: **one `.sort()` in a component** makes the directory an advertising surface without touching the backend, the comment or the drive. A live dev provider read *"sponsored until 2026-11-03"* — `services-drive.mjs` set it to test the guard and never cleaned up, which is also how its check came to be testing the wrong half. **Now:** off both DTOs so a write is a **400 naming the property** (`forbidNonWhitelisted`), out of the payload and the model, and **existing values cleared as residue** — nothing set them deliberately, nothing can set them now, nothing reads them, and a date there asserts a commercial arrangement that does not exist. The honest migration, not the convenient one, as in Phase 7j. **The column stays**: a cheap bet in Phase 4, still cheap. The old one-check test became four — refused on PATCH, refused on POST, absent from the landlord payload, and the ordering guard now tested against a row that **really is sponsored**, set in the database because the API cannot, with the precondition asserted and the drive cleaning up. Falsified by reintroducing all three bugs at once: four failures, each naming its own. ⚠️ **And a fault in my own new smoke check, caught before it shipped**: written `if (type=="array" …) else false end`, it **passed on a 401 body** — a non-list went down the success branch. Third time in this repo, second in this file, which warns about it in its own header. Both it and the identically-shaped `checkedByAdminId` check now require the list and check every row, verified against five crafted bodies. 💰 **Selling placement is still undecided and still needs two things**: a label saying which entry is paid, and a contractor who can be billed — the identical blocker as lead fees, so it is now one decision, Outstanding §16 | ✅ **Found, measured, closed and falsified; 24 drive checks, 2 new smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20261004213000_sponsorship_residue/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/services/{services.service.ts,dto/service-provider.dto.ts}`, `frontend/.../core/models/service-provider.model.ts`, `scripts/{services-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.31, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §16 |

A nav that says you are in five places tells you nothing about which. **Now:** a **four-layer scroll shadow**, chosen because it is self-regulating — the cover layers scroll with the content over shadow layers pinned to the element, so an edge fade shows only while there is really more nav past it; a static gradient would promise more nav at the end of the strip. The scroll was fine; the measurement was early. ⚠️ **`ng serve` stopped watching silently mid-phase** and a working fix read as broken — the trap is in CLAUDE.md and I still spent a cycle on it; and `pkill -f "ng serv[e]"` matched the shell that also carried the start command, exit 144. **Not changed, and named:** two of fourteen items is what fits at 360px with word labels. | 75 | 🔴 **A phone-only account could lock itself out of the product permanently, with one typo.** Phase 7g gave a WhatsApp-first person their own account — no email, no password, the verified number as the single credential — and bolted it onto screens written for accounts that have an address. Measured on a real one made through the product's own three-step flow: `PATCH /api/users/me {"phone":""}` → **HTTP 500** (the database CHECK refusing the row, so no sentence the person can act on), and `{"phone":<one digit out>}` → **HTTP 200, and the account is gone**. After that the account points at a number nobody holds with `phoneVerified: false`; phone sign-in finds accounts by *verified* number so it cannot find them; `forgot-password` needs an address they do not have; and asking for a sign-in code on the number actually in their hand answers **"a code is on its way on WhatsApp"** and sends nothing, because that reply is deliberately identical for a number with no account. **The product reassures them indefinitely while they are locked out.** 🔴 **Both ways off such an account were refused with "This account signs in with Google."** Both guards were `if (!user.passwordHash)`, written when Google was the only way to have no password; 7g added a second and nobody revisited them. Not merely a wrong sentence — adding an address is what lets a phone-only landlord pay the verification fee (Outstanding §11 refuses PayFast without one) and what makes the account recoverable if the number is lost, and that message was the only thing in the way. The settings screen could not have done better: nothing in its payload said which kind of account it was, so it rendered "Currently" followed by an **empty bold tag**, asked for a password that does not exist, and offered a form whose only possible answer was about Google. **Now:** the profile field refuses to touch the number when it is the only way in (400 with a sentence, naming what to do first); a new `phone_change` token type with a `newPhone` column makes a change **proven before it lands** — the code goes to the new number, the account keeps the old one until it comes back, so a typo cannot be stored as the way in, the same shape as `newEmail` beside it; the confirmed number arrives **already verified in the same write**, because the un-verified window *was* the lockout; a single-use `consumeStepUp` code is the step-up for an account with no password (`confirmVerification` could not be reused — it also makes a different claim); both guards branch on `authProvider === 'google'` so a real Google account still gets the real message, which the drive asserts; `hasPassword` is derived onto `/auth/me` **and** the sign-in payload, never the hash; and the screen says "Add an email address", "Set a password", shows a WhatsApp code box where a password box was, and names the number as the only way in. **30 API + 25 UI checks**, falsified by reintroducing five bugs at once: **13 failures**, including the original 500 and the original 200-on-a-typo reproduced exactly as measured. ⚠️ **Three faults of my own, none caught by the check that should have caught it.** (1) **I shipped a dead button and something else found it**: `passwordReady` was a `computed()` over a `FormGroup`, whose value is not a signal — so it cached `false` from the empty form and **"Change password" was disabled forever for every account that has one**. This codebase's own recurring defect, introduced while fixing another one. The new UI drive missed it because it checked that the screen still *says* "Current password", not that the button can be *used*; `account-lifecycle-ui-drive` caught it by trying to click. Both assert pressability now. (2) **`hasPassword` was only on `/auth/me`** — the screen defaults a missing value to true for compatibility, which is wrong for a brand-new phone-only account whose sign-in payload is a different shape, so it restored the very screen this phase fixes. **The UI drive caught it and the API drive could not, because the API was right.** (3) **Two skip messages blamed the wrong thing** — derivation against the number the drive started with meant a section-1 failure made the skip read "the code could not be derived… so JWT_SECRET does not match the API", which was false; a third blamed the secret when the route name was simply wrong. A diagnostic that sends the next person to the wrong place is the same fault as a check that passes for the wrong reason. ⚠️ **And the UI drive crashed instead of reporting** on a 429 from the sign-up throttle it had exhausted itself; it reports what the screen says now and makes **one** account per run with the session copied into a context per width, rather than five against a twenty-an-hour limit. 🔴 **Named, not built:** losing the number **entirely** — stolen phone, dead SIM — still has no route back, because every route needs the old number in hand. Recovering without it means proving identity to a person, which is a decision rather than a patch | ✅ **Found, measured, fixed, driven and falsified; 55 drive checks, 575 smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20261005090000_phone_change_and_step_up/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/auth/{phone-otp.service.ts,account-recovery.service.ts,auth.service.ts,auth.controller.ts,dto/*}`, `modules/users/users.service.ts`, `frontend/.../core/{models/user.model.ts,services/auth.service.ts}`, `features/account/settings/account-settings.ts`, `scripts/{phone-only-drive.mjs,phone-only-ui-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.33, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` |
| 76 | **Assisted sign-up, admin-recorded — and 29 inputs under the tap target, found by accident.** The last of 7g's three items needed almost no flow: `complete` already required `acceptTerms: true` from the request and recorded *when* against the row the handset proved, and a helper cannot reach that step without the code, which goes to the person's own phone. **Help reaches the handset; consent stops at the person.** What was missing was only the record of who helped. **Admin-recorded rather than open to any signed-in account**, because there is no agent role here and an open version is a way to send sign-up codes to arbitrary numbers **with somebody else's name against the record** — the same shape as admin-initiated closure in 7i. ⚠️ **The reply carries no code and no ticket, and that is the whole design**: an admin handed either could create an account in somebody's name and tick the Terms for them, and the record would say they were helped. The drive asserts the six digits appear nowhere in the response under any key, and the smoke suite greps the message for six consecutive digits as well, because `has("code")` cannot see a code under another name. One deliberate divergence from the public endpoint: **the admin IS told a number already has an account**, because withholding it makes assisting useless in the case it matters most — somebody who has an account and has forgotten needs signing in, not signing up — and an admin can already read the user list. The refusal still names nobody, **and the public endpoint is unchanged, which the drive asserts**. The per-number code budget is shared with the public path (an admin helping is still messaging a number that is not theirs); `assistedByAdminId` is SET NULL so an admin closing their own account does not delete the records of the people they helped; the admin's list drops the number once it became an account; and it lives on the admin overview rather than a new nav entry, which would be a new way to ship a dead link. The screen says both halves out loud — "the code goes to their handset, not yours" and "they have to accept the Terms themselves" — and that wording is a check, because an admin who does not know it will watch their own phone, decide it is broken, and start typing the landlord's details in themselves. **27 API + 21 UI checks**, falsified four-at-once to 11 failures. 🔴 **And then the UI drive measured the new field at 37px, so I measured the rest of the app: every text control under WCAG 2.5.8's 44px — 29 of 29, from 37px down to 30px on the board's own filter row**, across the board, login, register, phone sign-up, account settings, the listing wizard, my properties and the admin overview. v1.93.0 closed Outstanding §13 by making `.btn` 44px app-wide after the third local patch; **nobody looked at the inputs**. ⚠️ **The drive that should have caught it said it had**: `layout-ui-drive` prints "every control clears 44px" and `measureRow`'s selector is `button, a.btn` — a claim about controls measured over a subset of them, for three releases, in the drive written for this exact problem. It says "every **button**" now, and a new section 9 **sweeps** every visible text control on six screens at four widths, because a row-shaped check can only find this on the rows somebody thought to list. Fixed in the base rule rather than on the field that found it, for the reason v1.93.0 records; `min-height` so nothing re-wraps, normal specificity because a tap target is not a look. Falsified by removing the rule: **24 failures**, naming each control and its height. ⚠️ **Three faults in my own drives, and the second was the fix for the first.** (1) A one-shot `storageState()` does **not** survive a rotating refresh cookie: reused across four widths it worked at the first and was signed out at every one after, and without the login-bounce guard the sweep would have measured **the login form's two fields** and called the page clean. (2) Chaining the state then left the original page holding a token rotated away three contexts earlier, so a later section was signed out — and it sat on a click until Playwright's 30s timeout and **died with a stack trace instead of saying so**, the third crash-instead-of-report this session. (3) The new sweep signed in per page per width — eight extra logins against a thirty-per-fifteen-minutes limiter, and the drive died at section 18 reading as an auth bug, which is the trap CLAUDE.md names | ✅ **Built, driven and falsified; 48 drive checks + a 24-check sweep, 585 smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/20261005100000_assisted_signup/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/auth/{phone-signup.service.ts,phone-signup-admin.controller.ts}`, `frontend/src/styles/styles.scss`, `core/services/admin.service.ts`, `features/admin/dashboard/admin-dashboard.ts`, `scripts/{assisted-signup-drive.mjs,assisted-signup-ui-drive.mjs,layout-ui-drive.mjs,phone-only-ui-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.34, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §13 |
| 77 | 🔴 **Lost-number recovery — the most dangerous path in the product, and a CHECK constraint that passed on NULL.** The gap named in 7o and carried through 7p: Phase 7o made a number changeable with the new one proven first, which covers switching SIMs and covers nothing when the handset is gone, because every route needs the old number in hand. A person with no email, no password and no phone had no way back to their own rooms, ever. **Two proofs, neither sufficient alone:** a person proves identity to an admin offline and the admin records **what** was checked and **when** (named, dated outcomes — the `VerificationRequest` and contractor-check pattern), and **the new handset answers a code**, so an admin cannot type a number in and have it become the way in. The approval response carries no code, which the drive asserts. ⚠️ **The narrowing that matters most:** `open` is **refused outright when the account has an email or a password**, naming the safer route — then a password reset does the same job and nobody has to be trusted. The admin screen refuses it in those words ("Do not use this") and does not even render the form. ⚠️ **What it cannot do, stated rather than implied: it cannot warn the real owner in time.** The notice is written the moment a request opens, to every channel the account has — and in this case there is no email and they cannot sign in to read a Notice, so the warning lands only after the fact. Nothing can fix that; a person with no email, no password and no phone has no channel left. The mitigation is the **record** — permanent, attributable, reconstructable — plus the completion notice naming both numbers and what to do if it was not them, and the **old number is retired** so the stolen handset cannot sign in. A **second admin approval** is the standard control and is deliberately not required, because this platform is run by one person and a two-admin rule would make it unusable by the only admin there is; recorded as a decision, one column if the team grows. 🔴 **And two of the six CHECK constraints passed on NULL.** Written `CHECK ("refusedAt" IS NULL OR length(btrim("refusedReason")) > 0)` — and `btrim(NULL)` is NULL, `length(NULL)` is NULL, `NULL > 0` is NULL, and **a CHECK that evaluates to NULL is satisfied.** A refusal with no reason went straight in: the exact row the constraint existed to refuse, on this path. `id_check_has_a_note` had the same hole and **no test at all**, which is why it slipped beside the one that did. Found because the drive tests the **constraints** with SQL, not only the service that also enforces them — the service's checks were right throughout, and a rule only the service holds is one direct UPDATE from being no rule. Both compare to NULL explicitly now, both have NULL and blank cases. `room_viewings_meeting_place_not_blank` shares the idiom and is sound only because its column is NOT NULL: luck, and recorded as such. **The layering proved itself under falsification**: with the service's identity guard removed, approving returned **500**, because the database still refused the write. **50 API + 29 UI checks**, falsified in two passes (five bugs, then three isolated) to 13 and 3 failures. 🔴 **Then the UI drive's whole-page button sweep found eleven sub-target controls, none of them on my screen** — the language switcher at **24px** (smallest in the app), `.filter-pill` at **22px** (the board's own filters), the save-heart at 32px on every room card, **`.nav-burger` at 32px — the only navigation a phone has**, the cookie "Got it" at 30px on every first visit, the filter drawer's toggle and close, the desktop `.portal-nav-link` at 37px including Log out, and **`.auth__submit` at 38px: the primary button on every auth form**. The board had **25 of 34 buttons under target**. Fourth pass at this family — v1.93.0 did `.btn`, 7p did the inputs after 29 shipped under target, and each fix was called app-wide while reaching only the selector it named. ⚠️ **7p's own fix moved the hole rather than closing it:** it added a text sweep while `assertRow` kept measuring buttons a row at a time, so a button outside any listed row stayed invisible. A whole-page **button** sweep runs beside it now; falsified by reverting the rules, 18 failures. ⚠️ **One near-miss recorded:** `.auth__submit` measured 38px, cream background, grey text, and has no CSS rule anywhere — which reads exactly like `btn-ghost-light`. It was measured on an **empty** form where the button is correctly disabled; filled in it is terracotta on white. The height was the only defect, and reporting the rest would have been a fabricated finding | ✅ **Built, driven and falsified; 79 drive checks, 590 smoke** | `backend/prisma/migrations/{20261005120000_lost_number_recovery,20261005130000_recovery_check_null_hole}/`, `schema.prisma`, `modules/auth/{lost-number.service.ts,lost-number.controller.ts,dto/lost-number.dto.ts,auth.module.ts}`, `frontend/src/styles/{_spec.scss,_responsive.scss}`, `shared/components/{cookie-consent,lang-switcher}`, `features/auth/{lost-number,login,auth.routes.ts}`, `features/admin/{recoveries,admin.routes.ts,admin-nav.ts}`, `core/services/admin.service.ts`, `scripts/{lost-number-drive.mjs,lost-number-ui-drive.mjs,layout-ui-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.35 |
| 78 | **The four documents a landlord keeps asking for: lease agreement, renewal addendum, move-in inspection, deposit receipt.** Free to download, print and sign. ⚠️ **Two of these are contracts and two are not, and that distinction runs through the whole feature.** An inspection is a checklist and a receipt is a receipt: clumsy wording inconveniences somebody. A lease and an addendum create binding obligations, and a wrong clause harms a landlord with four back rooms who trusted us. So `reviewState` is on the DATA, not in a comment: the two contracts ship `awaiting_legal_review` against the same open attorney review `/legal/sublet` needs, and say so on their own face. They are still downloadable, deliberately — a landlord who cannot get one here takes a worse one off a search result with no warning on it at all. What they must not do is look finished. 🔴 **The check that matters most: a warning that disappears when the document is printed is a warning the person holding the paper never sees** — and printing is the entire point. The drive applies the print stylesheet with `emulateMedia({media:'print'})`, the same cascade the browser uses to make the PDF, and asserts the warning is still visible; same for the footer disclaimer (not a law firm, nothing signed here, no deposits or rent held) on all four. Falsified by `display:none` on `.tpl-warn` in the print block plus two more bugs: **8 failures**, the key one reading "the person holding the paper never sees it". **No PDF library was added** — the browser has one, every Android phone reaches it through Chrome's Print → Save as PDF, and it renders fonts correctly on a device nobody here can test; a server-side renderer is a dependency, a font-licensing question and a layout we could not check on the handset this is built for. So the whole job is a **print stylesheet**, and the site chrome is hidden **by selector globally** (`app-navbar`, `app-footer`, `app-cookie-consent`, `.portal-nav`) rather than by a class each page remembers — a printable page that forgot the class would print the navbar and the cookie notice onto a lease, which is the exact shape of defect Phase 7e was about. **Public rather than portal-only**: somebody searching for a South African lease template is a landlord with rooms and no tools. The highest-value one is the **inspection**, and it is also the lowest risk — the Act makes the joint inspection statutory and treats a landlord who skips the outgoing one as having agreed the room was fine, so no deduction; a landlord who skips it has already lost the deposit argument and usually does not know it. The **deposit receipt** carries the thing small landlords routinely do not know (the deposit earns interest and the interest is the tenant's) and ⚠️ **is explicitly the landlord's own receipt to their tenant** — Mastande holds no deposits, and a template that read otherwise would cross the money-custody line this codebase refuses to cross. Statutory references kept deliberately few: padding a template with section numbers makes it look authoritative, which is the opposite of what an unreviewed document should look like | ✅ **Built, driven and falsified; 45 drive checks, 596 smoke** | `frontend/src/app/features/templates/{template-content.ts,templates-list.ts,template-document.ts,templates.routes.ts}`, `app.routes.ts`, `styles/styles.scss`, `shared/components/footer/footer.ts`, `features/landlord/landlord-nav.ts`, `scripts/templates-ui-drive.mjs`, `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` §5.36, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §8 |
| 79 | 🔴 **The preflight passed on production, then the migration landed on localhost and reported success.** The production API was down on `P2022 rooms.listerType does not exist` — 15 migrations behind. The preflight was run against production and was right about everything: 15 pending, named the column, named the contractor side effect. The next command was the one the preflight's own closing line printed, `cd backend && npx prisma migrate deploy`. Twelve migrations went to `localhost:5432/rentboard_dev`, the output read **"All migrations have been successfully applied."**, both commands exited 0, and the same `P2022` was in the production log minutes later. ⚠️ **Two causes, both already documented in this repository.** (1) An inline `VAR=… cmd` prefix applies to that one command and does not carry across `&&`. (2) **`prisma migrate` connects through `directUrl`, not `url`** — `schema.prisma` declares `directUrl = env("DIRECT_URL")`, so exporting only `DATABASE_URL` leaves `DIRECT_URL` coming from `backend/.env`, and the run goes local **even with the production URL in the environment**. I proved this the wrong way round first: exporting `DATABASE_URL` alone and watching Prisma still print `rentboard_dev`, I concluded `.env` overrides the process environment. It does not — exporting **both** works, and the measurement that looked like precedence was the second variable. 🔴 **And `scripts/migrate-remote.sh` already refused every part of this**: unset `DATABASE_URL`, unset `DIRECT_URL`, a localhost target, a `-pooler` host as the direct one. Its header comment describes the *staging* version of this exact mistake, and §5b documented it. **I wrote §5 without reference to either and told the operator to call `prisma` directly** — a safeguard that exists, works, and was routed around by the document telling somebody what to run. All four of its guards re-verified by tripping them. **Now:** a §0 blocks when `DIRECT_URL` is unset, **naming the host `backend/.env` would have sent the migration to instead**, and blocks when the two URLs resolve to different databases, or to two databases sharing a name but not a migration history; the closing instructions hand over `migrate-remote.sh`. ⚠️ **Three more defects, every one of them legible in the output the operator pasted back, which is the only reason they were found — I had read that output and not seen them.** (1) It reported **"29 applied, 15 pending"** of 34, which does not add up: it counted `_prisma_migrations` rows, and production holds 10 names absent from this checkout. Those are named as their own warning now and the three numbers reconcile by construction. (2) It warned **"0 contractor(s) are listed and ALL of them will be un-listed"** and counted that nothing toward "1 thing(s) to know about first" — a ⚠️ that fires on an empty table is how an operator learns to skim the real one. (3) `?schema=public` is Prisma-only and `psql` rejects the whole URI over it, so **the script could not run against development or staging at all**; it worked on production only because that URL carries `sslmode` and `channel_binding`, which libpq happens to understand. A preflight runnable in exactly one of three environments cannot be rehearsed before it is trusted. 🔴 **Worst of the lot: the replica I validated the original script against was not a replica.** I had built it by deleting migration rows from a fully-migrated database without dropping the objects those migrations create — so §2's "nothing would fail" was measured against a schema ahead of its own migration history, and the rehearsal on it died `P3018 relation "phone_signups" already exists`, an error production cannot produce. The real one is built by applying production's own 19 migrations to an **empty** database. On that: all 15 applied, `rooms.listerType` and `users.walkthroughSeenAt` appeared, no row left unfinished, 3 seeded contractors came out un-listed and the one `sponsoredUntil` date cleared, with 3 DB-only rows present throughout to prove they do not block the run. ⚠️ **`migrate status` exits 1 on those rows** — verified by running it rather than asserted, because the warning tells the operator to expect it. (2) **`q()` returns `{error}` and the code treated it as a string.** `String({error})` is `"[object Object]"`, so a failed read went into the applied-migration **Set** as a migration name, was compared as a row count, and was interpolated into a blocker. Every number in §1 was derived from a failed query and **none of it looked like an error** — the whole family of defect this file exists to record. Three false statements about the place they were sitting in. The inbox reads the tenancy, `/applications/mine` returns it, and the dashboard gained **"Where you live now"** plus a derived sentence in place of the hardcoded one. ⚠️ **My first fix invented the fault it was fixing:** a new kind `tenancy_unconfirmed` for "accepted, not yet confirmed" — except accepting **opens a pending tenancy in the same transaction**, so the acceptance branch was unreachable and the new kind was a branch nothing could run. `dashboard-ui-drive` caught it; one row now carries both outstanding things. 🔴 **"The needs you tab does nothing and the page looks the same":** `landlordNav` has pointed at `#needs-attention` since Phase 7a and the panel renders nothing when nothing needs doing — each defensible, together a dead control. Measured before the fix: `hasAnchor: false`, `scrollY` 0 before the click and 0 after. **The nav file's own comment describes this exact fault three lines above the item.** It now renders when the fragment asks, says in words that nothing is waiting, and scrolls itself into view — the router's `anchorScrolling` looks once, at NavigationEnd, before the inbox request has come back. 🔴 **"I can't go back to the dashboard without the burger menu":** `.nav-inner.is-signed-in .nav-actions .btn-ghost { display: none }` hid **every** ghost action; a tenant's only two are Dashboard and Log out, so their header held a logo and a burger. ⚠️ Keeping Dashboard cost **21px of horizontal scroll at 360px** for a landlord (193px of budget, 227px of buttons) — fixed by tightening the signed-in header the way the visitor's already was; a tenant fitted with room to spare, which is how it would have shipped unnoticed. **The check that should have caught it passed for four releases** because of what it *says* rather than what it does: "the phone header is not asked to carry Dashboard, List a room AND Log out" only ever looked for Log out. 🔴 **"The rest are just bad UI layout":** `.field`, `.field-label` and `.btn-link` — **baselined as debt in Phase 8c and left**. `.field` is a `<label>`, so captions sat beside their controls and the public-page bio was a `<textarea>` at its default 20-column width; `.btn-link` fell through to the bare-button default, rendering **every stored document as a terracotta primary button**. | 89 | **WhatsApp switched off — a per-head cost on a free product, and the two things that would have gone wrong quietly.** Meta bills **per message**: about **USD $0.0095 + VAT** per sign-in code to a South African number, from the first one, with **no free allowance** (the 1,000-a-month free tier is service messages only). On a product that is free to list and free to apply that is a cost that scales with users and not with revenue, so `WHATSAPP_ENABLED` now defaults to **false** — including when the line is missing. ⚠️ **Switched off, not deleted:** the listing bot, parser, webhook, signature verification and landlord opt-in all remain, and one variable brings them back; deleting a thousand working lines and rebuilding them in six months is waste. 🔴 **Two decisions were put to the owner rather than assumed.** `sendOtp` is the **only** delivery for a phone code — there is no SMS provider, and even **assisted sign-up**, where an admin sits with the landlord, sends over WhatsApp and deliberately does not return the code in the response — so switching it off closes sign-up, sign-in, number change and lost-number recovery for anybody with no email address, which `User.email` is nullable precisely to support (*"many township landlords are WhatsApp-first and do not use email at all"*). Chosen: keep the flows, disable delivery, and **say so on screen**. And `wa.me` links cost nothing — they open the person's own WhatsApp — so sharing a room and tapping "WhatsApp" on a plumber stay, with a drive guarding them against a later over-eager sweep. 🔴 **The rent reminder would have lied in the database:** `reminderSentAt` is stamped whether or not the send succeeded, and that stamp excludes a period from the next pass — so every unpaid month would have been marked reminded while nothing was sent, and would **never be reminded again, including after WhatsApp came back**. A silent permanent hole created by a feature flag; the pass now returns before the loop. 🔴 **And the notices screen would have accused itself:** a false `sendToNumber` makes the notice router write *"WhatsApp declined the message…"*, which renders as "WhatsApp could not deliver this one" against every notice ever written — a reason that is false, because nothing was declined and nothing was sent. It now returns before the attempt so the column stays null, which is the distinction it was added to preserve. ⚠️ **Every guard refuses BEFORE a code is issued**, not after: issuing one nobody can receive still burns the per-number budget, still writes a row, and still leaves somebody watching a handset. Falsified by removing the gates — a real billable template message lands on the stub Cloud API and the drive prints the payload | ✅ **Built, driven and falsified; whatsapp-off-drive 17 checks, whatsapp-template-drive 21 (now explicitly the ON path), smoke asserts BOTH states and keeps the no-enumeration guarantee inside the 503, otp/phone-signup drives stop loudly rather than skipping quietly; production build clean, backend tsc clean, env-parity green** | `backend/src/config/configuration.ts`, `backend/src/modules/whatsapp/whatsapp.service.ts`, `modules/auth/{phone-otp,phone-signup,lost-number}.service.ts`, `modules/notifications/notice-router.service.ts`, `modules/properties/rent.service.ts`, `frontend/.../core/config/feature-flags.ts`, `features/auth/{login,register,lost-number,auth.routes}`, `scripts/{whatsapp-off-drive.mjs,whatsapp-template-drive.mjs,otp-drive.mjs,phone-signup-drive.mjs,smoke-test.sh}`, `docs/WHATSAPP-SETUP.md`, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §29 |

Text on dark is now `--white`, which is what it always was; the ground is `--cream2` lightened to #F4F2ED, so a **white card lifts off the page** instead of sitting flush on it. `--card` is gone — a card is `--white`. ⚠️ **Contrast moved in the safe direction and that is measured, not assumed**: every text token was computed against cream or the old card, and both grounds got *lighter*, so each ratio in the comments is now a floor rather than a reading — `a11y-drive` confirms it across **10 public + 23 portal pages**, every text node over threshold at rest, on hover and on focus. The one thing that got worse is `--border` on white (1.3:1), which is why **`--border-strong` (#8E8679, 3.55:1)** now draws the edge of anything a person types in: WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1 for a control boundary and `--border` has **never** met it on any surface this app has had — it did not show while the card was #FDFAF4 and the input was white, because the FILL was the boundary and the border was decoration on top of it. A white card removed the fill and left the border doing a job it could not do. 🔴 **And `.figure` was a design-system class with a paragraph of rationale and zero callers.** styles.scss has carried the display-vs-ledger rule since Phase 8 — display face for a number a person looks FOR, mono only where a column has to line up — and nothing ever called it, so every figure in the product rendered in the platform monospace while the system said otherwise. The room price was mono **and** `--terra`: the most important number on the board, set in the face for code and the colour of the primary button, competing with every Apply on the page. It is now Playfair 900 in ink. Five rules call **one `@include display-figure`** rather than writing the four properties out again, because the alternative was tried and is what row 90 is about. `.app-rent` and `.arc-month__amount` stay on `--font-mono`: alignment is the job there. ⚠️ **Three gates caught my own drift during the change** — `1.3rem` off the font scale (→ 1.25rem), and two literals that duplicated a token only *after* the tokens moved toward them (#E3DED6 was a fallback for `var(--line)`, a token that does not exist; the two print-block greys joined the two already allowlisted, because a note fill on A4 is toner and would print as nothing if it followed a white screen surface). 🔴 **`css-coverage-audit` was already red before any of this**, from the survey work: four classes used in templates and styled nowhere — including **`.visually-hidden`, which was used before it was ever defined**, so the question prompt it was meant to hide from the screen rendered as ordinary inline text and appeared twice on every free-text step. | 101 | **§59's `plan: free` confirmed by the owner — and what that makes concrete.** The blueprint wrote `plan: free` on the production service to match staging, with a warning that it **must be confirmed against the dashboard, because declaring `free` on a paid instance downgrades it**. Confirmed on 20 October 2026: both services are on the free plan. The downgrade risk is gone and a blueprint sync can no longer change the instance class. The other field was never a guess — `JWT_SECRET` is `sync: false` deliberately, because a regenerated secret invalidates every live session and refresh token, as it signs them. 🔴 **The confirmation turns a hypothetical into a live one: the free tier sleeps, and production is on it.** After roughly fifteen minutes of no traffic the next request takes **30–60 seconds** to wake the service. `render.yaml`'s own header called that "a poor experience for real users" — while arguing production should therefore not be on Render at all, and production has been on Render, on the free plan, since §59 put it there. So the first person to open `umastande.co.za` after a quiet quarter-hour waits up to a minute for a room board, on a cheap Android, on mobile data. That is the person this product exists for. **A plan decision, not a defect**, recorded so it is one somebody MADE rather than one that happened. ⚠️ **And the blueprint header had been false for several releases**: *"There is NO PRODUCTION SERVICE defined here yet"* and *"production currently has no backend deploy path at all"*, while `rentboard-api` sat 170 lines below it with `branch: master`. A header that contradicts its own file is worse than none — somebody reading the top of `render.yaml` concludes production is not deployed from here and stops looking. Both corrected, and the free-tier consequence moved from an argument against doing a thing into a note about the thing that was done | ✅ **`env-parity` green, `render.yaml` parses, no code change** | `render.yaml`, `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §59 |
| 102 | **The keep-warm ping — and the production deploy nobody was verifying.** Render's free plan suspends a service after ~15 minutes idle and the next request takes **30–60 seconds**. A non-200 **warns rather than fails**, because a red cross six times a day is how a gate becomes one nobody reads. ⚠️ **It pings `/health/live`, a new endpoint that touches no database.** `/health` calls `prisma.isHealthy()` — correct for a READINESS check and wrong here: pinging it every ten minutes forever would hold the **Neon compute** open around the clock as a side effect of keeping the web service awake, paying a second bill to solve the first. 🔴 **And the route did not land where it reads**: `setGlobalPrefix`'s `exclude` matches exact paths, not prefixes, so with `'health'` alone `/health/live` returned **404** and the route was actually served at `/api/health/live`. Measured, not assumed. 🔴 **And `verify-deployment.yml` ran on `develop` only, carrying the instruction *"Add master here in the same change that adds one"* — §59 added the production service and never came back, so the last three releases went to production with nothing verifying them.** Now both branches, one job with a switch rather than two copies, **because the expectations invert**: staging must refuse every crawler and must not advertise production URLs; production must do the opposite. §59 names the reverse fault — production mislabelled `staging` refuses every crawler, which is catastrophic and **indistinguishable from a healthy staging box to a staging-shaped check**. Proved on all four combinations: both correct shapes pass, both mislabellings fail | ✅ **`workflow-lint` green on 5 files; the liveness route measured on all three paths before and after the exclude fix; the robots logic driven through four cases** | `.github/workflows/{keep-warm.yml,verify-deployment.yml}`, `backend/src/modules/health/health.controller.ts`, `backend/src/main.ts` |

| 105 | **Browse shortcuts, and the rule that they must be keepable.** The brief asked for a category row — "rooms to rent", "student accommodation", "accommodation by location" — with the constraint that decides the whole design: **only categories the listings actually support**. So they are derived, never written down. `GET /rooms/categories` groups live `active` rooms by type and by city, counts what is free within the fortnight, and **returns nothing with a count of zero**; each shortcut carries that count, so it is a promise with its evidence attached, and a category disappears when its last room is let rather than becoming a dead link nobody notices. The constraint is visible in the output rather than asserted in a comment: there is **no "private room" shortcut, because the board holds no private rooms**, and no hardcoded "student accommodation" for the same reason — offering one would be the same defect as the location autocomplete suggesting a town with nothing let in it, which is why `suggestLocations()` is drawn from the board too. They are **links**, not click handlers, so a shortcut is middle-clickable, shareable and part of history like any other search — which only works because row 104 made the URL the search state. A place shortcut goes through `?q=`, so it runs the same phrase parsing a person typing "Pretoria" gets, and shows the same removable chip. Loaded independently of the results, because a category row that shrank as somebody narrowed their search would be a second set of filters pretending to be navigation. ⚠️ **The route is declared ABOVE `@Get(':id')`.** Nest matches in declaration order, so a literal path after a parameterised one is never reached — `/rooms/categories` would arrive at `findOne()` as an id of "categories" and return 404. `@Get('suggest')` sits above it for the same reason. 🔴 **The drive asserts each shortcut delivers THE NUMBER IT ADVERTISES**, not merely something: a shortcut showing 253 and delivering 6 is wrong in a way `>0` cannot see. All eight match, all are 44px at 360/390/768/1280, and the suite is 41 checks. Falsified by injecting the exact defect the design prevents — a hardcoded `Student accommodation 12` for a type with no listings — which failed with `"Student accommodation 12" -> 0 rooms — promised 12`, after a restart that proved the bugged build was the one answering. | 112 | 🔴 **`GET /api/rooms?page=999999999999&limit=999999999` returned 500 on the busiest unauthenticated route in the product** — found by the new API contract sweeps. `skip = (page - 1) * limit` is about **1e21**, far past what Postgres takes for an OFFSET, so Prisma threw and the board answered Internal Server Error to an anonymous caller. **Either parameter alone was fine**, which is why nothing had ever hit it. The unbounded `limit` was the second half of the hole — no maximum at all, so one request could ask for every active row on the board. `@Max(100)` on limit, `@Max(100_000)` on page; the pair bounds the product so skip cannot exceed 1e7. Measured: 500 → **400 "limit must not be greater than 100"**, boundaries exact (100 and page 100000 pass, 101 and 100001 refused, `?page=1&limit=12` untouched). Falsified by removing both decorators — the 500 returns and two tests fail. ⚠️ **And the inventory the sweeps run on was wrong in the one direction that HIDES routes.** It reported `POST /admin/recoveries/confirm` and `GET /landlord/storefront/:slug` as guarded; both are **public**. Two files declare TWO `@Controller` classes each, and the parser read only the first prefix and the first class-level `@UseGuards`, so the second controller's routes inherited the first one's guards — the real paths (`/auth/lost-number/confirm`, `/storefronts/:slug`) were never called at all. Nothing downstream could catch that; the sweep did, on its first run, because a guarded route answering 404 to everybody is not a thing that happens. Public is 41, not 39. | 10 | ~~Missing `track` in `@for` loops~~ | ✅ **Already fixed** | Verified by grep — every `@for` in the repo has `track`, zero exceptions |
| 11 | ~~No error boundary on lazy-loaded routes~~ | ✅ **Fixed in v0.9.3, guarded for SSR in v1.75.2** | `withNavigationErrorHandler` added to `app.config.ts` — a failed chunk load now triggers one hard reload of the attempted URL (resolves the common stale-deploy case) with a `sessionStorage` guard against looping if the underlying route is persistently broken, not just a stale chunk. That recovery is browser-only and the handler did not say so: it ran during SSR too, where `sessionStorage` and `window` do not exist, so a server-side navigation failure threw a `ReferenceError` out of the handler meant to contain it. See row 27. |

## Third-party credentials needed for a real deploy

| Service | Env vars | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Supabase Postgres | `DATABASE_URL`, `DIRECT_URL` | Pooled (6543) vs direct (5432) — see `README.md §19` |
| Google OAuth | `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_CALLBACK_URL` | Optional — email/password login works without it |
| ImageKit | `IMAGEKIT_PUBLIC_KEY`, `IMAGEKIT_PRIVATE_KEY`, `IMAGEKIT_URL_ENDPOINT` | ✅ **Configured and verified 26 Aug 2026** — uploads confirmed working end to end. `IMAGEKIT_URL_ENDPOINT` must match `imagekitUrl` in the frontend environment files exactly, or every image 404s |
| Resend | `RESEND_API_KEY` | ❌ **Not set.** The API boots and logs `[email skipped]` instead of sending, so nothing breaks — but a landlord is never told an application arrived, which breaks the core loop in practice. Highest-value remaining credential |
| WhatsApp Business API | `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, `WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` | Optional — email notifications never depend on this. `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` is the Meta App Secret that signs inbound deliveries; without it the webhook refuses every one, so the listing bot is off rather than open |
| Stripe | see table above | Required for pass 0.8.0's payment flows |
| Vercel (frontend) | none | Vercel's Git integration deploys from the repo. `deploy-frontend.yml` was removed — it needed tokens nobody set and never deployed anything. |
| Render (backend) | none | Render deploys `develop` from `render.yaml` on push, no GitHub secret needed. Railway was dropped. |

## i18n — translation status (from §19, repeated here so it's not missed)

English is authoritative. Afrikaans and isiZulu are real attempts flagged for native-speaker review. **isiXhosa, Sesotho, Setswana, Sepedi, Xitsonga, siSwati, Tshivenda, and isiNdebele are English-fallback stubs** — the app runs correctly, but the language switcher cannot honestly claim to support those 8 until real translation happens.

## Temporarily disabled (by request, not a fix)

**Billing (Stripe + Renter's Passport) is paused.** Every landlord is on an unlimited free tier: unlimited listings, up to 20 photos/room, one-click relist. This is a deliberate, reversible flag flip, not a rollback of the v0.8.0 work — nothing was deleted.

| What | How it's disabled | To re-enable |
|---|---|---|
| Stripe module (checkout, webhooks, boosts, passport) | `StripeModule` import + registration commented out in `backend/src/app.module.ts` | Uncomment both lines |
| Room-count cap | `enforcePlanLimit()` commented out in `RoomsService`, call sites removed | Uncomment the method + both call sites (in `create()` and `relist()`) |
| Upgrade / Boost / Passport UI | Gated behind `BILLING_ENABLED` in `frontend/src/app/core/config/feature-flags.ts` | Flip `BILLING_ENABLED` to `true` |
| Upgrade / Passport routes | `billingEnabledGuard` redirects away while the flag is off | Same flag flip — the guard checks it directly |

**Two real bugs found and fixed while doing this work, unrelated to the disable itself:**
1. `UpdateRoomDto` never declared `heroImagePath`/`imagePaths` as valid fields, despite the create-room wizard sending exactly those on the photo-save step. With `forbidNonWhitelisted: true` globally, this meant **every photo save via the wizard should have been rejected with a 400** since pass 0.6.0. Fixed by properly declaring both fields with validation (`@ArrayMaxSize(19)` for the gallery, enforcing the new 20-photo cap at the same time).
2. **One-click relist had no UI at all** — the backend endpoint and the Angular service method both existed since pass 0.3.0/0.4.0, but nothing on any dashboard ever called `relistRoom()`. Built the missing UI: `LandlordDashboard` now shows let (archived) rooms with a working Relist button.

The 20-photo cap is enforced in three independent places: `PhotoUpload` (client, stops accepting uploads at the limit), `CreateRoomDto`/`UpdateRoomDto` (`@ArrayMaxSize`, rejects with a clean validation error), and `RoomsService.assertPhotoLimit()` (defense in depth, technically redundant given the DTO check but kept deliberately).

## Verification scripts

These exist so readiness can be re-checked rather than assumed. Run them after
any dependency change, schema migration, or deploy.

```bash
./scripts/smoke-test.sh          # 461 API checks (3 skip). See the release invocation below.
./scripts/verify-build.sh        # what the production build and deploy artefact SERVE
./scripts/test-imagekit.sh       # ImageKit credentials and signed upload

# Browser drives — need the API on :3000 and the app on :4200
node scripts/phase-drive.mjs           # every screen is reachable
node scripts/a11y-drive.mjs            # 25 pages: headings, names, contrast
node scripts/search-drive.mjs          # keyword search: URL state, chips, 4 widths
node scripts/mobile-drive.mjs          # the five bugs found on a real phone, at 390px
node scripts/lease-drive.mjs           # lease windows and notice, as dates
node scripts/lease-ui-drive.mjs        # the yard panel, and both Phase 4 cards together
node scripts/services-drive.mjs        # directory filtering and number normalisation
node scripts/services-ui-drive.mjs     # tel:/wa.me links, errors not reported twice
node scripts/refund-drive.mjs          # the refund promise
node scripts/inbox-drive.mjs           # the task inbox's ORDERING, and what 5d refuses to quote
node scripts/inbox-ui-drive.mjs        # it leads the dashboard; every row acts and is distinctly named
node scripts/storefront-drive.mjs      # published only when asked; slug collisions; the sitemap entry
node scripts/storefront-ui-drive.mjs   # that a CRAWLER is served content, not "Loading…"
node scripts/notes-calendar-drive.mjs  # a private note is private — even from an admin; calendar dates are days
node scripts/lease-docs-ui-drive.mjs   # both parties see the lease; nothing claims it was signed here

# Needs its own API, pointed at the stub it starts (see the script's header)
node scripts/storage-drive.mjs         # that deleting a file REALLY deletes it
```

**The release invocation.** Anything less than this leaves checks unrun:

```bash
ADMIN_EMAIL=<seeded admin> ADMIN_PASSWORD=<their password> \
  WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET=<the API's own> \
  RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET=<the API's own> \
  ./scripts/smoke-test.sh
```

with the API started against real `IMAGEKIT_*` credentials, the frontend running
on :4200, and the database seeded including `SEED_LAUNCH_CODES=true` and
`npm run seed:survey -- --apply`. That is 432 checks; the suite prints
this list itself whenever anything skipped.

`storage-drive.mjs` is the one that must not be skipped before a release. It is
an ImageKit stub, so it checks a `DELETE` actually arrived for the right file and
that a storage failure is **not** recorded as a deletion — the promise in the
privacy policy, the PAIA manual and the tenant upload screen. Run it with all
four `IMAGEKIT_*` variables set as its header says; set three of four and
`smoke-test.sh` goes red on an unrelated check.

Set `ADMIN_EMAIL` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD` for `a11y-drive.mjs` or it skips the nine
admin screens — and **a green run over empty screens proves nothing**: an
accessibility run passed 23 pages while the admin queues had no rows, and a real
heading defect on a populated queue survived it.

**`verify-build.sh` cannot reach zero skips in one run.** The development bundle
bakes in `http://localhost:3000`, so the room and sitemap checks need an API
there, while the hanging-API check has to black-hole that same port and needs it
free. Run it both ways before tagging. It counts and names every skip rather than
reporting "safe to tag" over them.

`smoke-test.sh` creates real rows using timestamped test emails, so it is safe
to run repeatedly against a dev database. Never run it against production.

## Outstanding product gaps (not bugs)

| Gap | Notes |
|---|---|
| ~~"How it works" and Pricing pages~~ | ✅ **Built.** Both are routed in `pages.routes.ts`, carry SEO metadata, and prerender — verified in the build output. This row said "no routes exist"; that has not been true for some time |
| "Back to all rooms" | Returns to the board but loses scroll position and filters |
| Saved rooms persistence | Device-local (localStorage), correctly scoped per user since v1.4.2. A join table plus two routes would make it follow the account across devices |
| ~~Renter's Passport~~ | ✅ **Built in v1.56.0 and free.** Identity plus any one income proof earns the badge, which a landlord sees on every application with the checks behind it one tap away. Free is the product decision, not a gap: "free to apply, always" is the position against the incumbents, and charging the side of this market with the least money to prove they can afford a room would be the fastest way to lose it. The R89/month subscription page it replaces has been deleted |
| ~~Tenant alerts~~ | ✅ **Built end to end.** `SavedSearch` model, CRUD endpoints, and matching that fires on publish and relist as a single database query rather than in memory. The `daily` digest **does** exist — `AlertsDigest` in `modules/alerts/alerts.digest.ts`, `@Cron('0 7 * * *')` at Africa/Johannesburg, registered in `AlertsModule` with `ScheduleModule.forRoot()` in `AppModule`. The tenant dashboard has the Room alerts section: list, empty state, per-search description and pause toggle. This row previously said the digest did not exist and those tenants received nothing — that was wrong, and it is the kind of error that makes the project look less finished than it is |
| ~~Tenant alerts for new listings~~ | ~~**Not built.**~~ No saved searches, no digest, no push. A tenant must return to the board and re-run their filters. This is the single biggest retention gap: most rooms are let within days, so a tenant who checks weekly misses everything. Needs a SavedSearch model, a scheduled matcher, and email/WhatsApp delivery |
| ~~Landlord verification~~ | ✅ **Built end to end.** `VerificationRequest` model with an admin review queue. Approving an identity check is the only thing that sets `LandlordProfile.idVerified`, so the badge means something. Documents upload privately (`isPrivateFile`) and are **deleted on decision** — only the outcome is kept, per POPIA s.26. Both UIs exist: `features/landlord/verification/landlord-verification.ts`, routed at `/landlord/verification`, and `features/admin/verifications/admin-verifications.ts`. This row said neither was built |
| ~~Landlord verification~~ | ~~**Not built.**~~ `LandlordProfile.idVerified` exists as a boolean and is shown on room cards, but nothing sets it — there is no document upload, no KYC provider, no admin review queue. The disclaimer already tells tenants listings are unverified, which is honest, but the flag implies a check that does not happen |
| ~~Post-tenancy disputes~~ | ✅ **Built in v1.56.0.** Either party can report a problem once a tenancy has ended. An open report reduces the account's visibility — a flagged landlord's rooms rank last — and does not hide listings, close applications or suspend anyone, because a flag is an untested allegation until an admin reads it. Admin endpoints exist; a dedicated admin review screen is the one piece still outstanding, and the queue is readable at `GET /tenancies/flags/open` until it is built |
| ~~Multi-room properties~~ | ✅ **Built in v1.57.0.** A six-room yard was six unrelated listings. `/landlord/yard` groups them, rolls vacancy and waiting applicants up per property, and carries manual rent tracking. Grouping is optional and deleting a yard never deletes its rooms |
| ~~WhatsApp-first listing creation~~ | ✅ **Built in v1.58.0.** A verified landlord sends photos and a sentence to the Mastande number and a draft is waiting on their dashboard. Nothing sent over WhatsApp goes live: the draft becomes a Room in `draft` status only when the landlord claims it on the web, and it still has to pass the ordinary publish rules. Matched on `phoneVerified`, never on `User.phone` alone. The parser is regex and the Place taxonomy, not an LLM — a third-party model would mean personal information leaving the country under POPIA s.72 and would work worst in the languages that need it most. See README §24 |
| ~~Verification fee tied to the new checks~~ | ✅ **Done in v1.59.0.** The R149 is a landlord identity check and nothing else — no listing fee, no application fee, and every tenant proof free, all asserted in the smoke suite rather than trusted. The fee now appears on the verification's own audit trail, and rejecting a paid check sets a refund obligation automatically with a queue on the admin dashboard: the pricing page has promised "refunded in full" since launch and nothing in the code kept it. Boosts and subscriptions remain paused and board advertising is unchanged. Gaps M1–M3 in `docs/FLOW-AUDIT.md` |
| ~~Rent tracking — tenant side~~ | ✅ **Built in v1.71.0.** `/tenant/rent` shows what the landlord recorded and lets the tenant answer it, which is what the overdue reminder has been telling them to do with nowhere to do it. The answer sits beside the landlord's record and stops further reminders for that month. Gap Y3 closed |
| **The portal sidebar was hidden on mobile with nothing replacing it** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.71.0.** `.portal-nav` was `display: none` below 768px and the top hamburger carries only public links plus a Dashboard button — so on a phone every portal screen except the dashboard was unreachable: Rent, the Renter's Passport, My property, Verification, Settings. On a product built for people on cheap phones that is most of the app. Now a horizontal scrolling strip with 44px targets |
| Students-welcome filter | **Removed from the UI.** There is no students flag on the Room model or the filters DTO, so the checkbox could never narrow anything — and it was counted in `activeFilterCount()`, so ticking it told people a filter was active while the board stayed identical. Offering a control that does nothing is worse than not offering it, the same call already made for untranslated locales and the paused pricing links. To build it: a boolean on `Room`, the landlord wizard, the filters DTO, the query, then the checkbox back with a translated label |
| **The rent ledger could be written for months outside the tenancy** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.116.0 (Phase A of the lifecycle work).** `RentService.mark()` refused `cancelled` and nothing else, so a landlord could record rent against a tenancy that ended two years ago, a month before the tenant moved in, or next March — `upsert` created the row with `amountCents` snapshotted off a tenancy that was not running. The invariant is **not** "the tenancy is active": a rent period belongs to the months the tenancy covered, so a finished letting stays correctable (a tenant moves out on the 30th owing that month; the landlord records it on the 5th). `assertRentWindow` bounds the months instead. `dispute()` is deliberately NOT bounded — the final month is the most consequential mark a tenant receives and `TenancyFlag.unpaid_rent` can rest on it. `history()` now answers `{ tenancy, periods }` so a screen can tell a finished letting from a running one. Proof: drive §7, 18 checks, each guard reverted and confirmed to fail (7 red, then 3 red, then 57 green) |
| A tenant cannot give notice | **Open, deliberately.** The route's OpenAPI summary said "by either side" while `LeaseService.giveNotice` calls `assertLandlordOwns` — corrected to say landlord-only rather than relaxing the guard. Admitting the tenant needs `Tenancy.noticeRecordedById`: `noticeGivenById` records who notice is *attributed to*, not who entered it, so there is no safe rule for who may withdraw it, and a landlord clearing a tenant's notice resets a countdown that frees a room. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §30 |
| A tenancy is opened fire-and-forget | **Open.** `ApplicationsService.accept` calls `createFromApplication(...).catch(...)` unawaited so it cannot fail the acceptance, and nothing retries — if it throws, the application is `accepted` with no tenancy and nothing would notice. It also made the drive flaky; the drive now polls, the fire-and-forget stands. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §30 |
| **An unchecked `http.get` generic hid a crash on the landlord's property screen** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.116.0.** The rent endpoint has two consumers and only one was migrated to `{ tenancy, periods }`. The generic on `http.get` is an unchecked cast, so `RentPeriod[]` over an object passed `tsc` AND `ng build --configuration=production`, while the yard stored the envelope and `currentLabel(periods)` called `.find` on it. `RentLedger` is now declared once in `core/models/property.model.ts` and imported by both — adding it made the yard an immediate compile error. ⚠️ A new `yard-layout-drive` section reads the rendered label, because the obvious checks cannot see this: with the bug back, a `pageerror` listener stays green (Angular catches template errors) and the control count stays green (an object is truthy). Only the content check goes red — measured at 2 failed, 39 passed |
| **`updateTerms` could null an active tenancy's `startDate`** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.116.0.** Harmless until the rent window read `startDate`, then unrecoverable: `mark()` says "Confirm the move-in first" and `confirmStart()` refuses because the tenancy is already active, so neither route can restore it. It was also untrue — `confirmStart` sets status and startDate together, so an active tenancy with no start date is unreachable through the API. Now refused, along with a future date on an active tenancy |
| Drives were fabricating a tenancy the API cannot produce | ✅ **Fixed in v1.116.0.** Three drives forced `UPDATE tenancies SET status='active'` and left `startDate` null. The guard exposed it; they now set both columns. Also fixed: `inbox-drive` indexed the envelope so its tenant-dispute block was skipped with no failure, and `dashboard-drive` asked one tenant for `application_accepted` AND `rent_unrecorded` — mutually exclusive, since the acceptance row only exists while a tenancy is `pending`. That one was pre-existing (2 red before Phase A, 2 red after the fix) |
| **A tenant could not give notice on their own home** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.117.0 (Phase B).** The route documented "by either side" from the day it shipped while `LeaseService.giveNotice` called `assertLandlordOwns`, so a tenant got a 403 — a documented capability the code refused. It needed `Tenancy.noticeRecordedById` first: `noticeGivenById` is who notice is *attributed to*, not who *entered* it, so a landlord logging "my tenant told me" was indistinguishable from a tenant's own act and there was no safe withdrawal rule. Now: whoever recorded it may withdraw it, a landlord may record either side, and a tenant may only record their own (claiming the landlord served them notice is a different fact and not theirs to assert) |
| **`ended` was doing two jobs** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.117.0.** "Ended last week, reviews open, both parties still acting" and "ended in 2024, read-only" want different screens and permissions and had one value. `Tenancy.archivedAt` splits them — a column, not a derived `reviewsCloseAt < now()`, because derived state with many readers is how this repo got three nav definitions that disagreed. Written by the nightly pass once the review window closes, and immediately by `cancel()` (a cancelled letting never gets a `reviewsCloseAt`, so it would otherwise sit unarchived forever). ⚠️ Archived does NOT mean frozen: a tenant may still answer a rent month, per OUTSTANDING §30 |
| **Reports were not scoped to a letting** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.117.0.** `Report` carried `roomId` and `reportedUserId` only, so "what went wrong during this tenancy" was unanswerable and a complaint by one tenant was indistinguishable from the next tenant's two lettings later. `Report.tenancyId` is nullable (most reports come from people with no tenancy), `SetNull` not `Cascade` (a room's safety history outlives any one letting), settable only by a party to that tenancy — both parties read it in their archive — and **never backfilled**, because inferring which letting a historical complaint belonged to would manufacture facts |
| The nightly review/archive pass had no operator route | ✅ **Fixed in v1.117.0.** `POST /reviews/release-closed`, admin-only, mirroring `POST /properties/rent/run-reminders`. A cron nobody can trigger cannot be driven, checked after a deploy, or re-run when it fails — the only evidence it works is that nobody has complained, which is how a rent-reminder control ended up on a screen with no route. Returns `{ archived, released }` even at 0/0, so an operator knows it ran |
| Nothing renders `archivedAt` yet | **Open, by sequence not oversight.** The column has a writer and no reader until the archive view (Phase F of `docs/TENANCY-LIFECYCLE-AUDIT.md`). Recorded rather than implied away — a column with no reader is half the defect this repo keeps producing |
| Pre-existing schema drift makes every migration diff noisy | **Open.** `prisma migrate dev` offers five unrelated index drop/recreates (two on `users`), confirmed pre-existing by diffing with the pending changes stashed. Stripped from the Phase B migration by hand — dropping an index on `users` is a real operation and does not belong in a feature migration. Needs one migration of its own plus a runbook line. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §31 |
| **Ending a letting told nobody, and left the room invisible** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.118.0 (Phase C).** `end()` wrote four columns and that was all it did: the room stayed `let` indefinitely — off the board, out of the sitemap — and of 32 `Notice.kind` values not one was for a letting starting or ending. Now both parties get `tenancy_ended`, worded for who actually did it and naming the 30-day review window, and the landlord gets `room_needs_relisting` **while the room is still let**. A prompt, not an auto-relist: relisting republishes old photos at an old price and archives every open application, and that is the landlord's call |
| **A letting that fell through left the room invisible for good** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.118.0.** Accepting sets the room `let` and nothing set it back, so a room **nobody ever moved into** went off the board permanently. `cancel()` now restores it to `active` — not a relist, because there is nothing to archive: the other applicants were already auto-rejected at accept time and stay rejected, which is what happened |
| **`closureReason` asserted a relist that may never have happened** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.118.0.** It read "This tenancy ended and the room has been relisted" for a branch reached whenever a landlord relists a room they had let to this person — after an ended tenancy, after one that fell through, or after a letting never recorded. It asserted one of three as fact; it now says what is true in all of them |
| ⚠️ No EMAIL when a letting ends | **Open, and named rather than implied.** `NoticeRouter.deliver` emails when it can and then writes no in-app notice, so passing no email function is what guarantees the notice exists for everybody — including the phone-only landlord, whose only channel it is. The cost: somebody who does not sign in within 30 days **misses the review window**, which is the one notification here with a deadline. Needs a `sendTenancyEndedEmail` template and the two channels thought about together. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §33 |
| **The Reserved status has no button anywhere** | **Open.** The route, the API write, the tailored application refusal, the board badge and five separate counts all exist and work; no control in the portal can set it. So a landlord with somebody lined up has only "mark as let", which closes and emails every other applicant — the destructive action for a non-destructive reason. The frontend service also had two identical methods for that one endpoint, which is what happens to code nobody calls; one is deleted. ⚠️ The lifecycle audit's §D3 wrongly said nothing wrote the status — corrected in place. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §34 |
