| **The rent screen showed a finished letting as a running one** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.119.0 (Phase D) — the defect the owner reported.** It looped over active, ended and pending tenancies with no branch on which, so a letting that ended two years ago had the same heading, the same present-tense "R3 000/mo", the same "tell us if a month is wrong" banner and the same paperwork offered as current. Now two sections, the past one in the past tense ("Past letting", "Lived there 4 Jun to 1 Sep", "Rent was R1 800 a month"). ⚠️ The ledger and the answer button both STAY — previous payments must not disappear, and the final month marked after somebody leaves is the mark that matters most. The change is what the screen says, not what it permits |
| **Moving out moved the room backwards through the tenant's dashboard** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.119.0.** "Your applications" excluded only `active`, so an ENDED tenancy fell back in — the Application stays `accepted` and nothing archives it — and the derived note read "The landlord has moved on this one — it is waiting on you" about a room they left six months ago, while it vanished from "Where you live now". Now a whitelist (`tenancyHasMovedOn`: anything but `pending` is out) rather than a blacklist, because the first attempt at the fix dropped the `active` exclusion and reintroduced the original defect — the drive caught it |
| A past letting vanished from the dashboard entirely | ✅ **Fixed in v1.119.0.** Excluding finished lettings from "Your applications" left them matching NO section, since the application stays accepted and unarchived. One line with a link to the record, because a room somebody used to live in disappearing without trace is the same complaint as a hidden way back |
| **Backticks in inline templates — now a gate, not a note** | ✅ **Added in v1.119.0.** `scripts/template-literal-lint.mjs`. A backtick inside a component's inline `template:` literal terminates the template; `TS1005: ',' expected` then points hundreds of characters past the cause. Eight occurrences in this codebase, two of them in this phase, and the CLAUDE.md note has not been enough. Verified: exits 1 naming the file and line, exits 0 clean, 83 templates checked |
| ⚠️ `tsc --noEmit` does not typecheck templates | **Known, now recorded.** Passing `headingLevel="4"` to an input typed `2 \| 3` is caught by `ng build` and by nothing else. A clean typecheck says nothing about a component's inputs — worth remembering before trusting one |
| **A role button's text was invisible on hover (1.17:1)** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.119.1.** Pre-existing, found by the a11y gate being red rather than reported. `:where(button):hover:not(:disabled)` sets `color:#fff` at specificity (0,2,0) because `:where()` contributes zero; `.auth__role:hover:not(.is-active)` is (0,3,0) so it won the BACKGROUND and overrode it to cream — while saying nothing about colour, leaving the global white on top. White on cream is 1.17:1. **Overriding a `:where(button):hover` background without also overriding its colour is how a control gets invisible text in one state**, and the resting state measures 15.6:1 and says nothing about it. Also `.auth__role-sub` at .8 alpha over terracotta was 4.34:1 against the 4.5 it needs; .9 gives 5.07:1 and keeps the hierarchy |
| ⚠️ The a11y gate needs demo rooms seeded to be trustworthy | **Known.** Without `SEED_DEMO_ROOMS=true` it reports "the board had no rooms" plus an H2-before-H1 on a drive-fixture room — two artefacts beside any real finding, which is how a gate gets skimmed. Seed before believing it |
| **A landlord lost the tenant the moment a letting ended** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.120.0 (Phase E) — the mirror image of Phase D.** `properties.service` asked for tenancies `['pending','active']` and that was the ONLY place a landlord could see a tenancy, so an ended letting took the tenant, the dates, the rent paid and the ledger with it. No past-tenant view existed anywhere in the product, while the PAIA manual tells the public this platform holds "tenancy history and rent records". Now collapsed past tenants per room — who, the date range, what they paid, their paperwork, and whether a review is still open |
| **A room could sit `let` with nobody in it and nothing said so** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.120.0.** The status chip read "let", which is the opposite of help, and the Phase C notice fires once. Now a marker on the room carrying the relist action, plus a `room_vacant` inbox TASK — a notice fires once, a task persists until the state changes. Its urgency rises the longer it sits (an empty room is lost rent every day), floored so it never outranks a tenant disputing their rent record. Derived from `tenancies: { none: … }` rather than "is there an ended tenancy", so it also catches a room marked let by hand with no tenancy behind it. A paused room draws no task — `pause()` exists for exactly that |
| **Reserved finally has a button** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.120.0.** Closes `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §34. The route, the API write, the tailored application refusal, the board badge and five occupancy counts were all built and working with no control anywhere, so a landlord with somebody lined up had only "mark as let" — which closes and emails every other applicant |
| ⚠️ The initial bundle grew 1.5 kB, and it was already over budget | **Open — a decision, not a defect.** 456.42 → 457.92 kB, exactly the `+1,501 bytes` of new global CSS (measured by stashing). The budget is `maximumWarning` so it does not fail the build, but this is the first phase to move it the wrong way. The yard's styles are global by convention and `_spec.scss` records why (component styles out-specify the global sheet and silently broke the responsive layer once). Moving them to the yard's lazy chunk would fix the budget and reopen that. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §37 |
| ⚠️ The tenancy drive is now one run per API restart | **Known.** Eleven sections register ~35 accounts against 60/hour counted in memory; two runs back to back fail with a 429 that reads like an auth bug. Noted in the drive's own header |
| **The archive — a complete tenancy record, finally on a screen** | ✅ **Added in v1.121.0 (Phase F).** `/account/tenancies`, one list for both roles because a sub-lessor lets one room and rents another. Everything it shows was already in the database with nothing reading it, while `/legal/paia` told the public this platform holds "tenancy history and rent records". Carries the letting's facts, the whole rent ledger chronologically, published reviews, paperwork, how it began, and anything you reported |
| ⚠️ **The archive's permission rules are not uniform, and must not be made uniform** | **By design.** Six private tables, one payload, two readers. Reviews only once published (the double-blind rule — a held review read early turns a rating into a negotiation); tenancy flags and reports only the ones YOU raised (the schema calls showing an unreviewed accusation to its subject this platform's largest defamation exposure in South Africa); landlord notes **not at all**, for either party including their author, because this is a shared screen; no document storage path, ever. Each reverted and confirmed to leak — 7 red. ⚠️ The lifecycle audit's §F table wrongly said disputes were visible to both; the code follows the existing stricter rule |
| The a11y gate now covers the archive for both roles | ✅ **v1.121.0.** 19 portal pages, not 17. A new screen outside the gate's list is a new screen the gate cannot see — worth remembering for the next one |
| ⚠️ Inline component styles cost the initial bundle nothing | **Measured in v1.121.0.** The archive added ~2 kB of CSS and `styles.css` is byte-identical: inline styles land in the route's lazy chunk. The initial bundle grew only 154 bytes. Phase E's 1,501 bytes went into the global sheet by the yard's convention — this is direct evidence that moving those rules would recover all of it. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §37 |
| **A room could be relisted with somebody living in it** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.122.0 (Phase G).** `relist()` checked the ROOM's status and nothing about who was in it, and `let` passes that check. Measured: 200, room back to `active`, **on the public board**, tenancy untouched — tenants applying for an occupied room, which is `ReportReason.already_let`, the complaint the report enum exists for. Worse, `relist()` archives every open application and bumps `relistCount` on the way, so it is not undone by setting the status back; and Phase E's empty-room task cannot see it because the room is now `active`. **And the landlord was pointed there** by their own inbox. Now refused, naming whose move-out is missing. `pending` counts too — an accepted applicant is somebody expecting that room |
| Once a notice period elapses, both inboxes change their action | ✅ **v1.122.0.** While the countdown runs, relisting is the right thing to get ready for; once it has run out the step that unblocks everything is recording the move-out, because the room cannot go back on the board until it exists. Landlord: "Record the move-out" → `#moving-in-out`. Tenant: "Say you have moved out" → the same panel, because sending them to browse leaves a letting open behind them and the next thing they hear is a rent reminder for a room they left |
| ⚠️ An elapsed notice must never end a tenancy by itself | **By design, and asserted.** Where somebody lives is a record of fact; inferring a move-out from a date is how the product starts asserting things it does not know — the same reasoning that keeps the countdown computed on read and never written. The drive checks the tenancy is still `active` with a null `endDate` after the period runs out |
| ⚠️ The lifecycle audit's §C8 described the wrong defect | **Corrected in place.** It said an elapsed notice "produced nothing" and that no job watched it. Both inboxes already escalated it with "This date has already passed" — the handling is computed per read on purpose, which `LeaseService` says in its own header. Rent reminders continuing is also not a defect: if nobody recorded a move-out the person may still be there. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §39 |
| **Fourteen borders that were never drawn** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.123.0 (Phase H).** `var(--line)` was referenced 14 times across 9 components and defined **nowhere**. An unresolved custom property is not a CSS error — the declaration is dropped and `border-style` falls back to its initial `none`. **Measured in Chromium:** `1px solid var(--line)` → `style "none", width "0px"`; `var(--border)` → `solid 1px`. So the storefront ran as one unbroken column, the lease panel had no rule above its sections, the private-notes panel had no left bar and every task row touched the next. `ng build` succeeds (an unknown custom property is valid CSS), `tsc` never reads a `styles:` block, and `css-coverage-audit` asks whether a CLASS has a rule, not whether the rule RESOLVES. All 14 now say `var(--border)` |
| A literal colour that duplicates a token fails the build | ✅ **v1.123.0.** `scripts/token-lint.mjs`, in `npm run audit` and CI. Within **ΔE 3** (CIE Lab, not hex distance) of a defined token and it fails, naming the file, the line, the token and the ΔE. The 59 known uses sit in `scripts/token-baseline.json` keyed by file AND value AND count — so a new file reaching for `#FFF` fails, and so does a twentieth `#DDD5C8` in a file that already had nineteen. `--update-baseline` refuses to write a baseline worse than the one on disk |
| 57 literals migrated onto tokens | ✅ **v1.123.0.** `#FFF`/`#FFFFFF` → `var(--white)` (22 uses → 59) and `#DDD5C8` → `var(--border)` (131 → 145), across 20 files. `#DDD5C8` is 2.40 ΔE from `--border`: a difference nobody has ever seen, stored twice, where only one copy moves when the palette does — and this palette moves (`--terra` twice, `--gold-text` twice, `--slate` once, all for contrast) |
| ⚠️ `_spec.scss` keeps 13 of the baselined literals | **By design.** Its own header forbids hand-editing it: it is a verbatim port of the visual preview, so an edit here is lost on the next re-sync and the gate goes red again. They stay in the baseline rather than in an allowlist, so a re-sync that introduces a **new** off-token colour still fails — which is the signal that the preview grew a colour the token set does not have |
| ⚠️ Two print-stylesheet literals are allowlisted, with reasons | **Two entries, the only two.** `template-document.ts` lines 244 and 246: paper is paper, not a themed surface, and a token could be redefined where a sheet of A4 cannot. The other 59 are a ratcheting baseline instead, because an allowlist without reasons is where defects go to be forgotten |
| The migrated borders are PAINTED, at 360 / 390 / 768 / 1280 | ✅ **v1.123.0.** `scripts/token-render-drive.mjs`, 51 checks on two real screens — the public storefront and a landlord's private notes. Those two carry the only width-changing cases (`.badge` all four sides, `.notes-body` `border-left: 3px`); the other twelve are `border-top`/`border-bottom` hairlines that change height and cannot overflow. The notes panel is **clicked open** first: a panel nothing opens is a panel no check has ever measured. A selector matching nothing is a **failure**, not a skip |
| ⚠️ Both new gates were proven able to fail | **Five reintroductions, each reverted.** One migrated `#fff` back → named with its ΔE. A fourth `#3A3228` where the baseline allows three → `× 4 (baseline allows 3)`. `--update-baseline` with that literal present → refused. `var(--bordre)` typo → named file, line and name. `STYLES` pointed at a directory with no tokens → exit 2, "Refusing to pass vacuously". And reverting one `var(--border)` to `var(--line)` turned the render drive red in eight places |
| ⚠️ `tokens.mjs` had a filter that could never fire | **Fixed.** The header claimed SCSS variable DEFINITIONS were excluded from the count. The pattern was `([-\w]+)` with `.filter(m => !m[1].startsWith('$'))` behind it — and `[-\w]` does not match `$`, so on `$color-cream-2: #F2EDE3;` the match began one character later and `m[1]` was `color-cream-2`. Twenty palette definitions were counted as literal colour USES, and the handoff's headline figure was wrong for the life of the file (159/332, actually 150/314) |
| ⚠️ `stripComments` cannot see a comment inside an inline `styles:` block | **Fixed, after it cost one wrong edit.** The block is a template literal, so once the backtick opens the stripper is in string state and leaves everything to the closing backtick — correct for a string. The design-handoff count never noticed because it EXTRACTS the CSS and strips the extract. The first token-gate pass read the `.ts` in place and rewrote `#fff` inside screen-hint's comment explaining why that very declaration exists. One wrong edit in 57, invisible to `tsc` and `ng build`. Both consumers now share `scripts/lib/css-values.mjs` |
| ⚠️ `scripts/control-contrast-audit.mjs` has never existed | **Comment corrected; gate still not written** (`docs/OUTSTANDING.md` § 41). `screen-hint.ts` asserted it "now fails on any rule that sets one of the pair without the other". A gate claimed in prose and never written is the same defect as a control that cannot fail, one step earlier. The defect it describes is real and reached a phone: `:where(button)` sets white on terracotta at zero specificity, so a class setting only `background` inherits white — white on white, 1.00:1 |
| ⚠️ `template-literal-lint.mjs` was written in Phase F and wired nowhere | **Fixed.** No npm script, no workflow, no hook. A lint nobody runs is the same thing as no lint. Now in `npm run audit` and in `ci.yml`, alongside the token gate |
| `.arc-review__by` had no rule at all | ✅ **v1.123.0.** Phase F shipped it; the review attribution inherited `--ink` and read as loudly as the review itself. `css-coverage-audit.mjs` found it on this phase's build — which means it was red at `cc12db1` and Phase F's gates were reported green without rerunning it after the build. Now `color: var(--slate)` |
| ⚠️ The initial bundle budget overrun is pre-existing | **Measured, not assumed.** Stashed the change, rebuilt: **458.07 kB** before, **458.19 kB** after. The 450 kB budget was already exceeded by 8.07 kB; this phase added 0.12 kB, because `var(--white)` is eight characters longer than `#fff`. `bundle-budget.mjs`, which measures gzipped initial JS per prerendered page, passes with 122.5 KB to spare |
| **The wordmark existed twice, and MA had nothing to pin it to** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.124.0 (Phase I).** The markup was written twice (`navbar.ts`, `footer.ts`) and the rules twice (`.nav-logo` / `.nav-logo span`, `.footer-logo` / `.footer-logo span`). The split was `Mas` / `tande`, so **MA** was part of an unnamed text node with no element of its own — there was nothing to hold a colour. Now one `<app-logo>` with `MA` in `.logo__ma` carrying `var(--logo-ma)` in the component's own scoped styles, which Angular rewrites with an attribute selector: more specific than any parent stylesheet, and declared rather than inherited, so a parent setting `color` moves the stem and cannot touch MA |
| ⚠️ The audit asked for a `surface` input; it was not built | **Deliberate, and better.** An input with two values has a wrong value, and the wrong value here renders cream on cream or ink on ink — the invisible wordmark the finding was about. The stem is `currentColor` instead: it follows the surrounding text colour, which is by definition right for the surface and is already measured there by `a11y-drive`. There is no input to get wrong |
| ⚠️ Three dark surfaces declared a background and no foreground | ✅ **Fixed in v1.124.0.** `.nav` and `.footer` are `background: var(--ink)` with **no `color`**; `.portal-nav` is `background: var(--ink2)`, the same. Every child needing light text has been naming its own colour one declaration at a time. That is the `:where(button)` defect one level up — and the first measurement after the swap proved the cost: with the foreground undeclared the stem came back at **1.00:1** on `--ink`, the invisible-text failure this codebase already shipped once as white text on a white button. All three now declare it, in `styles.scss` |
| `--logo-ma: #C4522C`, and the number was searched not chosen | ✅ **v1.124.0.** One fixed value must be legible on `--ink` AND on `--cream`/`--card`/`--white`. Every existing terracotta fails one side: `--terra` is 3.06:1 on ink, `--terra2` is 3.33:1 on cream, `--terra-deep` is 2.28:1 on ink. The best achievable minimum across those five surfaces is **3.92:1** — anything light enough for #1C160E is too light for #FFFFFF. #C4522C hits that ceiling and is the closest to the brand of the values that do (ΔE 7.6 from `--terra`, 8.7 from `--terra2`) |
| ⚠️ 3.92:1 relies on the wordmark being LARGE text, and that is asserted | **3:1 is WCAG's large-text threshold and it applies at 18.66px bold or more.** The logo ships at 1.6 / 1.5 / 1.25rem at weight 900 — 25.6 / 24 / 20px, so the smallest has 1.34px of margin. A fourth, smaller size added without changing the colour would be a contrast failure that looks like a styling tweak, so `scripts/logo-drive.mjs` measures the rendered size and weight at every placement and width rather than trusting it |
| MA is one colour at every placement and width, proven | ✅ **v1.124.0.** `scripts/logo-drive.mjs`, 76 checks, two placements × 360/390/768/1280. The eight values are **compared**, not asserted one at a time; MA is checked to differ from the stem (a rule pinning both halves still looks fine on a dark header while the requirement is quietly gone); contrast is measured against the first opaque ancestor; the accessible name is checked to contain the visible text (WCAG 2.5.3); and the drive sets `color` on the host from outside to prove MA does not move and the stem does |
| ⚠️ The logo drive was proven able to fail | **Three reintroductions, each reverted.** `.logo__ma` loses its token → 17 red, MA and stem both `rgb(250,247,240)`. `--lg` dropped to 1rem → "16px at weight 900, floor 18.66px bold" at 768 and 1280. `.nav, .footer` stop declaring a foreground → stem at **1.00:1** on `--ink` |
| ⚠️ The drive caught my own design claim being false, first run | **The header said the stem "inherits the surrounding text colour". It did not.** The logo's root is an `<a>`, and the global `a { color: $color-terracotta }` has specificity (0,0,1) on that very element, so `currentColor` resolved to `--terra`: 3.06:1 on `--ink`, passing the 3:1 floor by 0.06 and nothing like what was designed. `.logo { color: inherit }` fixes it — 16.77:1. Nothing but a measurement would have found it; the wordmark looked entirely plausible |
| ⚠️ The backtick trap, eighth time, where the lint could not see it | ✅ **Lint widened in v1.124.0.** Five backticked identifiers in a comment inside the component's `styles:` block terminated the literal; the production build produced twenty errors including `TS2304: Cannot find name 'color'`. **`template-literal-lint.mjs` read green over it** — it looked only at `template:` literals, and only the first per file. Now `template:`, `styles:` and every element of a `styles: [...]` array, at every occurrence: 141 literals across 85 files. Proven by running both versions against the same defect — old exit 0, new exit 1 |
| ⚠️ The audit's §G3 finding 4 was wrong | **Corrected in place; no wordmark added to the portal.** It said the portal shell has none and a signed-in landlord never sees the brand. `app.ts` renders `<app-navbar/>` above `<router-outlet/>`, so the global header and its logo are on every portal screen at every width. One was added and then removed: a second wordmark puts the brand on screen twice, and below 900px that sidebar is a scrolling tab strip where `.portal-user` is already hidden for taking room from navigation |
| `nav-ui-drive`'s overcrowding check now reads the component | ✅ **v1.124.0.** It queried `.nav-logo`, which would have gone red rather than silent — the right failure mode. The trap it now documents: `app-logo`'s host is `display: contents`, so the host's own rect is 0×0 and measuring it would report every header as a 0px logo. The selector is `.nav app-logo .logo`, scoped because the footer carries a second wordmark |
| ⚠️ The initial bundle grew 1.56 kB, and four rules are dead | **Measured: 458.19 kB → 459.75 kB.** A new component in the app shell is not lazy, because the navbar and footer are on every route. `.nav-logo`, `.nav-logo span`, `.footer-logo` and `.footer-logo span` are now dead rules in `_spec.scss` — about 0.2 kB — deliberately left, because that file is a verbatim port and hand-editing it turns the next re-sync into a conflict for 0.04% of the bundle. `bundle-budget.mjs` (gzipped initial JS per prerendered page) still passes with 122.1 KB to spare |
| **Four parallel state systems, three of them mine** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.125.0 (Phase J1).** The "Past letting" chip was written **three** times — `.arc-row__badge` (archive), `.rent-past__badge` (rent), `.yard-past__review` (yard) — differing only by `.12rem` vs `.15rem` of padding. `.status--accepted` / `.status--rejected` were declared identically in **both** `applicants.ts` and `applicants-inbox.ts` with raw hexes. Beside them a naming fork — `.status-accepted` (spec layer, one hyphen) vs `.status--accepted` (components, BEM), neither shadowing the other — and a fourth convention, `app-status status-{{ p.status }}`, on the rent ledger. Phases D, E and F wrote three of those duplicates. Now five meanings defined once in `styles.scss` |
| ⚠️ Two of those rules were under the contrast floor | **Measured: `.status--accepted` (#3D7040 on sage 15%) 4.12:1 over `--cream2`, `.status--rejected` (#D63B3B on red 10%) 3.46:1.** The chip is .65rem at weight 700, so the threshold is 4.5:1. Both failed, on two screens, for every release |
| ⚠️ …and `a11y-drive` could not see it | **It has never accepted or rejected an application.** Neither chip has ever been on screen while contrast was being measured — a page passes that audit by not containing the failing element, which is the five-vacuous-passes lesson inside the gate that exists to prevent it. The drive now accepts one and rejects another on a second room (second room so accepting does not turn the first `let`; second tenant because one applicant cannot be both) |
| ⚠️ The per-room applicants screen had never been audited at all | ✅ **Fixed in v1.125.0.** `LANDLORD_PAGES` is static paths and that route needs a room id, so `applicants.ts` was simply not in the list — and `/landlord/applicants` is a **different** component (`applicants-inbox.ts`), so auditing one never covered the other. Pushed onto the list from inside the seed, which is the only place that knows the id |
| A real heading defect, found the first time that screen was audited | ✅ **Fixed in v1.125.0.** H3 straight after H1 — "Decided (2)", "New and viewed", "⭐ Shortlisted". The portal shell supplies the only h1, so every section heading skipped a level. Three `<h3>` → `<h2>` |
| The five state meanings, each measured over four surfaces | ✅ **v1.125.0.** A translucent tint composites over whatever is beneath it, so each pair was measured over `--cream`, `--cream2`, `--card` and `--white`. Worst case: live `--sage-deep`/sage 12% **5.21**, waiting `--ink2`/gold 14% **9.64**, past `--terra-deep`/`--cream2` **6.73**, closed `--slate`/`--cream2` **4.74**, disputed `--terra-deep`/red 10% **5.89** |
| ⚠️ The obvious colour choices all fail | **`--gold-text` on its own gold tint is 4.10:1** — and `_spec.scss`'s comment claims it "clears both… 4.61:1 on the tint", which was measured against the tint over `--card` only; over `--cream2` it is 4.10, so `.status-pending` has been under the threshold on that surface all along. `--sage` on the sage tint is 4.29, `--terra` on the terra tint 4.38, `--slate` on any tint 4.05–4.23. Hence deep inks throughout, and `waiting` carrying its meaning in the FILL with near-black ink: no gold close enough to `--gold` to still read as gold clears 4.5:1 as small text (the nearest that does is ΔE 23 away and reads brown) |
| Colour is never the only channel | **By design.** Every chip carries a word — "Past letting", "Never started", "Disputed" — so the ink reinforces the meaning rather than conveying it (WCAG 1.4.1). That is also what frees the palette from having to make five maximally-distinct hues out of one warm family |
| The mapping is a typed function, not a string per template | ✅ **v1.125.0.** `core/models/state.model.ts`. Two copies of a mapping is the same defect as two copies of a rule: the inbox folded `withdrawn` in with `rejected` — the same red — so an applicant who **stopped** read as one the landlord had **refused**. A new status added to either union is now a type error |
| `.state-edge`: the rule, not the card (§H3's second identity move) | ✅ **v1.125.0.** A 3px rule in the state's own ink instead of another tinted rounded box. Both devices read the same two custom properties, so a meaning is defined once and rendered two ways, and the 480px behaviour (indent out, rule on top) moved out of `rent.ts` into the vocabulary. The rent ledger now marks a **disputed month** with it: that was prose with a raised hand in it, so on a year of rows the one month somebody queried looked exactly like the eleven they did not |
| ⚠️ The state lint was proven able to fail | **Four reintroductions, each reverted.** A sixth meaning invented on a screen (`state--expired`, named with file:line). A component declaring the treatment again (named with the properties it set). A declared name losing its rule (the chip would render unmodified grey). The lint pointed where nothing uses the vocabulary (exit 2, "refusing to pass"). Its own first run produced two false positives — it matched the tail of `landlord-trust__state--ok` — which turned out to be a real finding: both modifiers were in the CSS-coverage baseline as styled nowhere, and are now deleted (baseline 23 → 21) |
| ⚠️ `.pill--*` is deliberately NOT governed by the state lint | **A pill here is an attribute, not a state:** "✓ Verified", "🛂 Passport", "Couples welcome". Forcing those into `live / waiting / past / closed / disputed` would mean picking a state for "Couples welcome". Measured on the way past: `.pill--green` (#3D7040 on sage 10%) is 4.42:1 over `--cream2` and `.pill` is .6rem — under the floor on that surface, though it renders on `--card` (4.90) and `--cream` (4.80) today. Worth knowing before a pill moves onto cream2 |
| **`_spec.scss`'s "DO NOT hand-edit" was a phantom control** | ✅ **Header corrected in v1.125.0.** It said the file is a copy "so it can be re-synced when the preview changes". **`RentBoard-ZA-Visual-Preview.html` is not in this repository and never has been** — `git log --all` for it is empty, and README.md lists it under "Historical source documents… files that existed at a point in time". There is nothing to re-sync from. The file has been hand-edited in **22 commits** since the one-time port, one of them mine three commits ago (`8c94aed` added the whole `.yard-past*` block while citing that header as the reason not to touch the file) |
| ⚠️ I quoted that phantom rule twice to leave real defects in place | **Both now fixed.** Phase H left 13 literal `#FFF` in `_spec.scss` and Phase I left four dead `.nav-logo` / `.footer-logo` rules, each justified by a re-sync that can never happen. The literals are `var(--white)`, the dead rules are deleted, and the token baseline ratchets **59 → 31** |
| ⚠️ The backtick trap, three more times — and the widened lint caught all three | **Ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth instances, all in `styles:` or `template:` comments in this phase, all named by `template-literal-lint.mjs` before any build.** One of them had already done damage: a backtick followed by a comma looked like a legitimate close, so the lint passed, and the **token gate** — which reads the same `styles:` ranges — silently stopped seeing nine literals in the truncated remainder and wrote a baseline nine entries short. One blind gate quietly corrupted another gate's baseline. The lint now also requires that what follows a candidate close is plausible: `})`, `],` or another decorator key, with comments stepped over (six correct files failed before comments were handled — a gate with six false positives gets switched off) |
| ⚠️ **ELEVEN on-screen promises of a WhatsApp message that is never sent** | ✅ **FIXED in v1.128.0 — superseding the row below, which recorded them as handed over.** CLAUDE.md is unconditional: nothing on screen may promise a WhatsApp message while it is off, and `WHATSAPP_ENABLED` is false on both sides. Five screens say it anyway: `walkthrough.ts` ×4 (two onboarding cards **titled** "Messages reach you on WhatsApp too"), `messages.ts` ×2 (the hint explaining what the screen is for), `account-settings.ts` ×1 ("Used for WhatsApp notifications"), `register-phone.ts` ×1, `passport-verify.ts` ×1. Four of the nine are onboarding titles and bodies and one is the messages screen's whole premise — rewriting those is a decision about what the product claims to be, and WhatsApp-first is the stated goal merely paused for cost. `docs/OUTSTANDING.md` §45 lists what each would have to say instead |
| ⚠️ The drive guarding that rule checked FOUR hardcoded files | ✅ **Widened in v1.126.0.** `whatsapp-off-drive.mjs` asserted that `login.ts`, `register.ts`, `auth.routes.ts` and `home.ts` read the flag — a check structurally incapable of seeing the thing it exists for, which is this repo's signature defect. The rule is a claim about **every** template. It now sweeps every component, matches promise-shaped sentences, skips comments, and skips any file that reads the flag. Nine carried in `scripts/whatsapp-promise-baseline.json`, count may only go down. Proven: a new "We send you a WhatsApp when this changes." line → ❌ naming the file |
| Two WhatsApp promises were fixed, in the lines J2 was already editing | ✅ **v1.126.0.** `notices.ts` ended "— and on WhatsApp if we can reach you there" → now "This screen is the record — nothing is sent anywhere else." `applicants-inbox.ts` said "we will tell you on WhatsApp and in your notices when one does, **so you do not have to keep checking**" — the second half is the worse half, telling a landlord to stop checking the only screen the information appears on → now "a notice appears in your account when one does" |
| **Not one of 38 loading states was announced** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0 (Phase J2).** A paragraph replaced by content when a request lands is silent to a screen reader: no `role`, no `aria-live`. 81 live regions exist in this app, 68 of them `role="alert"` on field errors, and exactly 11 paragraphs carry `role="status"` — none a loading state in a template. **`axe` does not flag a missing live region, so `a11y-drive` has never reported this and never could.** Now one `<app-loading>` with `role="status" aria-live="polite"`, 38 call sites in 28 components |
| Three screens used ONE element for error, loading AND empty | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0.** `notices`, `messages` and `applicants-inbox` each had `<p class="{notices,msg,ai}-empty" role="alert">` doing all three. So "Loading…" was announced as an **alert**, and "we could not reach the server" looked exactly like "you have nothing" — which on an applicants screen means a landlord concludes nobody applied. Each screen's own comment says "Three states, not two" and the markup then used one element for all three |
| The whole-screen error had no error styling | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0.** It rendered as muted body text — the same defect `.form-error` had before `css-coverage-audit` found it. `<app-error-state>` carries `role="alert"`, the state vocabulary's edge treatment and `var(--danger)` |
| ⚠️ `--danger` did not exist, and eight components each typed a worse red | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0.** `$color-danger: #B23B3B` has been in `_variables.scss` from the start at **5.48:1** on `--cream`, but an SCSS variable is invisible to an inline `styles:` block (§H2), so eight components typed `#D63B3B` from memory: **4.32:1 on cream, 3.96:1 on cream2** at .82rem. Eight independent contrast failures caused by a token they could not reach. Now a custom property, and adding it immediately exposed a ninth literal that had been invisible because nothing matched it |
| `.link-button` was declared in four component scopes and nowhere global | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0.** The `.btn-danger` defect again — the one `css-coverage-audit`'s own header records. Four identical bodies, no global rule, so the fifth component to write the class got an unstyled `<button>`: default chrome, default padding, mid-sentence. Phase J2's error-state was the fifth and the audit named it on the first run. Now global, once; four copies deleted |
| ⚠️ `css-coverage-audit` read class names out of COMMENTS | ✅ **Fixed in v1.126.0.** The error-state component documents the three screens it replaced, markup and all, and the audit reported `.notices-empty`, `.msg-empty`, `.ai-empty` and `.link-button` as used-and-unstyled — four false positives from one explanatory comment, in the gate whose job is to be trusted about missing rules. It now reads `onScreenSource()`: JSDoc, `//` and HTML comments blanked, offsets preserved. Verified still able to fail on a real bare class |
| ⚠️ `feedback-lint`'s own first assertion could not fail | **Caught before it shipped.** It checked that `<app-loading>` carries `role="status"` by searching the whole file — and the component's header *explains* why it carries `role="status"`, so the check matched its own documentation. Verified by deleting the attribute: the lint stayed green. Comments are stripped first now, and deleting it produces two failures |
| The pre-commit hook runs the four fast gates | ✅ **v1.126.0.** `template-literal-lint`, `token-lint`, `state-lint`, `feedback-lint` — **0.516s for all four, measured.** None needs a build, an API or a browser. The backtick trap is why: thirteen occurrences now, five of them on 11 October in the phases that wrote and widened the lint for it. A CLAUDE.md note did not stop it; a CI job reports it ten minutes later; the hook reports it as it is written |
| ⚠️ **DM Mono has never been fetched — every money figure rendered in Courier New** | ✅ **Token corrected in v1.127.0.** `--font-mono: 'DM Mono', …` and `index.html` has **never** requested it: the Google Fonts link asks for Playfair Display 700/900 and DM Sans 400/500/600/700 and nothing else. So the room price, the hero stat, the rent ledger and the archive's month amounts have all rendered in Courier New or the platform monospace since the day the token was written — while `design-handoff/TOKENS-CURRENT.md` reported DM Mono as one of "three real faces". The token now names `ui-monospace, 'Roboto Mono', Menlo, Consolas, 'Courier New', monospace`, which is honest and tabular. **If you want DM Mono it has to be added to the font request** — naming it does not fetch it, and a third webfont family for the digits in a rand figure is a cost call on a metered-connection product, not a bug fix |
| ⚠️ Nothing in this repo could see that, and one of my own claims was weaker than it read | **The token resolves, so `token-lint` is satisfied; the build does not fetch fonts; and `getComputedStyle().fontFamily` echoes the DECLARATION back** — so a drive reading it "confirms" a font that is not there. Phase I's `logo-drive` reports `"Playfair Display", Georgia, serif` for the wordmark: that proves the declaration, not the glyphs. Its load-bearing assertions (the 18.66px bold floor, the measured contrast) stand; the family is not evidence and is not treated as such |
| ⚠️ Fonts cannot be checked in a browser in this container, and that is measured | **`"DM Mono"`, `"Playfair Display"` and two deliberately bogus family names all render at identical width here** — 292.03px for the same string at 48px. Only generic `monospace` and locally-installed `Courier New` differ. No webfont loads at all, because `fonts.googleapis.com` is unreachable through the agent proxy. So `font-lint.mjs` is a **static** check against the markup, which is where the truth is |
| A font token may not name a family nothing fetches | ✅ **v1.127.0.** `scripts/font-lint.mjs`, in `npm run audit`, CI and the pre-commit hook. Two assertions: every family in a `--font-*` token is requested or is a system family; and every weight used with `--font-display` is one that was fetched — a 900 that was never fetched is **synthesised**, and a synthesised heavy serif is the difference between a wordmark and a smeared one. Proven: DM Mono back in the token → named; logo at weight 800 → named; the font request stripped → refuses rather than passing |
| **28 radius values for 174 uses** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.127.0 (Phase J3).** The tokens existed and were used — `var(--r4)` 40, `var(--r8)` 21, `var(--r-full)` 18 — and beside them `8px` ×15 (which **is** `--r8`), `6px` ×13, `10px` ×10, `4px` ×6 (`--r4`), `12px` ×3 (`--r12`), plus `5px`, `2px`, `20px`, `999px`, `.4rem`, `.6rem`. Nobody chose that tail. Now **15 values for the same 174 uses** and every remaining one legitimate: the five tokens (147), `50%` for circles (8), `0` for square (3), the SCSS `$r*` layer (10), four one-side compounds |
| Mapped by what the element IS, not by arithmetic | **Controls → `--r4`** (every `6px`, `5px`, `2px`, `.4rem`); **small surfaces → `--r8`**; **panels → `--r12`**; **chips at .6rem → `--r-full`**, which also makes them match the state vocabulary's chip. ⚠️ I kept `--r8` where §H3 said "r4 and r12 and nothing else": 21 existing uses is not a rounding error, and 8px is a real step between a control and a panel — removing it would be a 21-site visual change to satisfy the letter of a sentence in an audit I wrote myself |
| ⚠️ Radius has zero tolerance; spacing is ratcheted | **Deliberate split.** The radius migration is done, so `scale-lint.mjs` fails on any off-scale value. Spacing is 60 values over 1073 uses — migrating it would be churn with real visual risk and nothing reviewable, so 39 off-scale values over 536 uses are baselined (`.6rem` ×101, `.4rem` ×66, `.85rem` ×45 leading) and the drift stops. Proven four ways: `7px`; a **compound** `var(--r4) 3px 0 0` (the laxer first rule let `2px 2px 0 0` through, which is how that value survived); a new `.47rem`; and one more `.6rem` than the baseline allows |
| ⚠️ The scale lint's own first run miscounted | **`.6rem` and `0.6rem` were counted as two values** (87 and 14), which made the tail look longer than it is and would have let a file switch spelling to get under a baseline. Normalised — **11 of the original 49 entries were a second spelling of another** |
| The money figure in the display face — §H3's first identity move | ✅ **v1.127.0 (Phase J4).** `.stat-box .val` (the hero statistic) and `.room-detail__price` take `--font-display` at weight 900, with `.figure` in `styles.scss` so the next display figure gets the same treatment without a fourth opinion |
| ⚠️ …and deliberately NOT the ledger figures | **§H3 is right about display figures and wrong about ledger ones, and the reason is tabular numerals.** The rent ledger and the archive's month list are columns of amounts that must line up: the monospace stack gives fixed-width digits and Playfair, a proportional serif, does not. A column of rand figures that does not align is worse than a generic one. So `.app-rent` and `.arc-month__amount` keep monospace, and `.dash-count` stays a label |
| `font-variant-numeric: lining` on both display figures | **Not cosmetic.** Playfair ships **old-style figures** by default: without it the 2 and the 5 in "R2 500" drop below the baseline like lower-case letters, which on a price reads as a rendering fault |
| **All eleven WhatsApp promises now read the flag** | ✅ **v1.128.0.** `walkthrough.ts` ×3 (two onboarding cards **titled** "Messages reach you on WhatsApp too"), `messages.ts` ×2 (the screen hint and `replyGoesTo()`, where **both** branches named WhatsApp and neither was true), `account-settings.ts` ×3, `passport-verify.ts`, `close-account.ts`. Gated on `WHATSAPP_ENABLED` the way `login`, `register`, `home` and `auth.routes` already do — **not deleted** — so the honest sentence shows while the channel is off and the WhatsApp one returns by itself when the flag flips. That is what CLAUDE.md asks for: paused, not abandoned |
| ⚠️ Verified both ways, on screen | **Flag `true`:** "Messages reach you on WhatsApp too" is back in the production bundle. **Flag `false`:** the landlord tour was walked card by card at 390px — all eight cards read, card 1 "A listing saves as you go", card 3 "Every conversation in one inbox", **no WhatsApp anywhere** — and settings shows "Used to verify who you are" with the WhatsApp hint absent |
| ⚠️ `account-settings` had a dead control, not just wrong copy | ✅ **Fixed in v1.128.0.** "Verify this number" calls `requestPhoneVerification`, which sends the code over WhatsApp, and the API refuses with a **503**. A button that can only fail is worse than no button, so while the channel is off the sentence says so and **the button is not rendered** |
| ⚠️ `passport-verify` told a tenant their referee had been contacted when nobody had | ✅ **Fixed in v1.128.0.** `ReferencesService` attempts WhatsApp, gets `delivered: false`, records `reference_send_failed`, and the admin screen tells an operator to **phone** the referee. The tenant-facing copy now says a person phones them, which is what happens |
| `register-phone` is exempt by a RULE, not a list | **The route is behind `whatsappOnlyGuard`**, which redirects to `/auth/register` while the flag is false, so its copy cannot be on screen while the promise is untrue. The sweep reads the guarded component paths **out of `auth.routes.ts`** — a baseline entry would be permanent debt for copy that is already correct, and a hardcoded exemption would go stale the moment the guard was removed. Proven: deleting `canActivate: [whatsappOnlyGuard]` makes that page's two promises fail immediately |
| ⚠️ The promise pattern had to be widened once | **The first version matched `account-settings.ts` on "Used for WhatsApp notifications" and missed two more claims in the same file.** A promise is not only "we send" — it is any claim that something will arrive **or that a WhatsApp-dependent thing can be done** ("you can sign in with a WhatsApp code") |
| ⚠️ The baseline is DELETED and the tolerance is zero | **`scripts/whatsapp-promise-baseline.json` existed for a few hours.** Because zero findings is now the correct answer, the vacuous-pass guard had to be rewritten to count **files scanned** rather than findings — otherwise "found nothing" and "is not looking" are the same result. Proven three ways: a new promise anywhere; the route guard removed; the sweep pointed where nothing mentions WhatsApp, which **refuses** |
| **A first-use hint that could never be dismissed** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.128.0 — a Phase F miss.** The archive screen shipped `key="account-tenancies"` and the key was never added to `SCREEN_HINTS` on the server, so "Got it" posted, the API answered **400**, the optimistic patch rolled back and the hint came straight back on the next visit. `screen-hint.ts`'s own header says it: *"a hint you cannot put away is worse than no hint at all."* `scripts/hints-drive.mjs` exists for exactly this and **I did not run it during Phase F** — it was found while verifying something else. Proven by removing the key again |
| ⚠️ **"by RentBoard ZA" under every house ad — and the script to fix it could not** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.129.0, three defects deep.** `ads.service.ts` returns `advertiser: campaign.advertiser.companyName` and `ad-slot.ts` renders `by {{ advert.advertiser }}` under **every** advert, so three house ads whose own copy had been right since the rename were each signed with the old brand. **My earlier advice to run `seed-house-ads.ts --apply` would have printed a calm success and changed nothing** — reproduced before fixing: it said *"Nothing to change — the house ads already match the code"* and left `RentBoard ZA` in place |
| ⚠️ The chain of three in the seed | **(1)** `prisma.advertiser.upsert` said `update: {}`, so `companyName: 'Mastande'` applied **only on create** — any database where the row existed kept its old name for ever (in `seed-house-ads.ts` *and* `seed.ts`). **(2)** the change detection compared four fields of the CAMPAIGNS and nothing of the advertiser, so the dry run could not see its own subject. **(3)** and because of (2) the `if (!changes) return` fired first, above the advertiser upsert — **so fixing (1) alone would have changed nothing** |
| ⚠️ Why no check in this repo could see it | **Every rebrand check here reads SOURCE.** This value is a database row: not in the code, not in a template, and the code that governed it was correct the whole time. `scripts/house-ads-drive.mjs` reads what the **API serves**, at all three placements (`board_sidebar`, `board_inline`, `room_detail`) — an advert is only on screen if something asks for its placement, and a sweep of one would have missed two. Proven three ways, including **0 adverts served → it refuses**, because every other assertion is a negative over served text |
| An unknown ad placement was a 500 | ✅ **Fixed in v1.129.0.** `?placement=board_grid` — the guess this drive made on its first run — went straight into a Prisma `where` on an enum column and threw inside the query. A bad query parameter is the caller's mistake and must read as one: a 500 says the server is broken and is indistinguishable from a real fault in a log. Now a 400 naming the valid values |
| **A terracotta retired for failing contrast was still in use, 15 times** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.129.0 (Phase K).** `#C04E28` was `--terra` until it measured **4.50:1 on `--cream`** — the AA minimum to two decimals, which subpixel rendering tipped to 4.49. The token moved to `#AD4222`; **fifteen translucent uses of the retired value stayed**, plus two of `#7A6E60` (the slate retired at 4.26:1). Seventeen in all, across six files: button lifts, focus rings, gradient stops, tinted backgrounds, card borders |
| ⚠️ `token-lint` was blind to them, by its own correct design | **Its header says so:** translucent `rgba()` is skipped because `rgba(28,22,14,.07)` cannot be replaced by `var(--ink)`. Right for substitution — and it leaves a hole, because nothing compares the **opaque base** of a translucent value against a retired one. Retinting cost was measured first: **−0.06 at worst** across all four light surfaces |
| ⚠️ A pre-existing margin the retint surfaced, unchanged | **Both tints sit under 4.5:1 on `--cream2`** — terracotta 4.38, slate 4.20 — carrying small text in `.tpl-flag`, `.ec`, `.status-rejected`, `.status-withdrawn`. True before the retint (4.43 / 4.21), which costs 0.05 of it. **Today they render on `--cream` and `--card`, where they pass**, which is why `a11y-drive` is green. Move one onto cream2 and it fails, and nothing would say so — the drive measures where things are, not where they might go |
| **18 shadows for 32 uses — three kinds of thing wearing one property** | ✅ **Fixed in v1.129.0.** `--shadow-sm/md/lg/xl` existed and were used 8 times; the other 24 were `none` ×11 and thirteen one-offs that were **not all elevation**: six focus/selection RINGS (a spread with no blur, at 1px *and* 3px, in four tints), four black overlay shadows where every token is ink-tinted, and a button lift. **18 values → 11**, same 32 uses, every one now a token, a composition of tokens, or `none` |
| Four `--ring-*` tokens, all 3px, one alpha | ✅ **v1.129.0.** A ring is not a shadow, so forcing it onto `--shadow-*` would have been wrong. WCAG 2.4.11 wants a focus indicator findable, and **a ring whose thickness depends on which control you tabbed into** is the kind of inconsistency nobody reports and everybody feels. Two were 1px; all four are 3px now |
| ⚠️ Two shadows were the same value in two spellings | **The overlay `0 18px 44px rgba(0, 0, 0, .22)` and the lift `0 4px 14px rgba(192, 78, 40, .35)` each appeared twice — once spaced, once not** — so the handoff counted each as two distinct values. The same defect as `.6rem` vs `0.6rem` in spacing |
| Shadows at zero tolerance, font size ratcheted | ✅ **v1.129.0.** `scale-lint.mjs` grew two dimensions. Shadows: zero tolerance, like radius, because that migration is done — and it checks **each part of a composition**, so `var(--ring-terra), 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.1)` fails. Font size: 49 values for 438 uses with the top ten inside a 0.28rem band, so 28 values over 222 uses are baselined and the drift stops |
| ⚠️ Three bugs in the scale lint's own first run | **`--update-baseline` refused a first write for a new dimension**, because every font size was above an empty map — the same first-write allowance `token-lint` already makes. **The refusal printed the wrong base map**, reporting "`.6rem` 101 → 6" by comparing a font size against the *spacing* baseline: a message that invents a regression out of two unrelated numbers is worse than no message. And **`.82rem !important` counted as a distinct value**, with four `clamp()` hero sizes reported as off-scale when a fluid size is a deliberate choice no fixed scale can express |
| Three red CI checks on the merge, separated before being fixed | ✅ **v1.130.0.** Compared step-for-step and annotation-for-annotation against the pre-merge `develop` head. `E2E` looked pre-existing by job name and was not: pre-merge it died at step 12 (a11y), post-merge at step 10, so a *different* failure wore the same name. `Backend` and `Staging configuration` were byte-identical before and after. §54 |
| The WhatsApp flag broke an e2e assertion nothing had re-read | ✅ **v1.130.0.** `a047c18` put "Verify this number" behind `@if (whatsappOn)` — correct, the control cannot work while the channel is off — and `account-settings.spec.ts:33` still asserted the button was visible. Now asserts the intent (saving does not verify) in a form that holds either way, with the button's count read from `WHATSAPP_ENABLED` rather than hardcoded. Reproduced, fixed, then the bug reintroduced (`phoneVerified \|\| phone`) and the spec failed again |
| ⚠️ Two lint errors predating this branch blocked the whole backend job | ✅ **v1.130.0.** `'code' is assigned a value but never used` (`phone-signup.service.ts:512`, from 5 October) and `no-useless-assignment` on `propertyId` (`rooms.service.ts:317`, from 3 October). Two lines, and they were costing the typecheck, the build, the 352-assertion smoke suite and the refund drive, all of which skip when Lint fails |
| ⚠️ A guard that reported on itself without having run | ✅ **v1.130.0.** The two environment-guard steps carry `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` so a test failure cannot hide a broken production config — but **Build** skips on a lint failure, so they ran `node dist/main.js` against nothing and reported *"the guard failed, but not on the placeholder API_URL"* and *"a correct production config did not pass the guard"* about a guard never invoked. Both now assert `dist/main.js` exists and say Build was skipped instead. Proven by moving the file aside |
| A diagnostics step that could fail on its own | ✅ **v1.130.0.** `API log on failure` `cat`-ed a `/tmp/api.log` the never-started API had not written, adding a fourth red row saying nothing about the fault. Falls back to a message now |
| ⚠️ `Staging configuration` is an operator item, not a code one | **Red before the merge and after it, same step and same annotation.** `STAGING_API_HOST` is set so the check enforces; it fails on `/health` not answering in eight minutes or on a version other than `1.81.0`, and root/backend/frontend `package.json` all agree on that number, so the check is sound. Needs the Render dashboard, unreachable from this container: either the deploy has not landed or the service is suspended |
| ⚠️ What CI still covers alone | **The backend has no unit tests** (`npm test` is an honest stub), so a green backend job never meant unit coverage. Every other backend step has now been driven locally: lint, both typecheck projects, build, both environment guards, the **616-assertion smoke suite** and the **18-check refund drive**. On the e2e side, step 10 (3 app journeys) and step 12 (the a11y drive, 40 pages) were driven here and are green in CI; the **mobile-board project**, **`phase-drive.mjs`** and the **production-build locale/SEO pass** are still CI-only. The staging deployment cannot be probed from this container at all |
| ⚠️ A cascade hides failures as well as inventing them | ✅ **v1.130.0.** Fixing the Lint error let **Typecheck** run for the first time and it failed: three `'u.email' is possibly 'null'` errors in `tsconfig.seed.json`'s project, both files byte-identical to the pre-merge head. Then the smoke suite ran for the first time and failed three more. **616 smoke assertions, an 18-check refund drive and a whole typecheck project had been dark behind two lint errors from 3 and 5 October**, and nothing in the job output said so. §55 |
| ⚠️ `inspect-board.ts` crashed on the account type it is most useful for | ✅ **v1.130.0.** `u.email.slice(...)` on a nullable column — and **39 phone-only accounts already exist in this database**, so the script threw `Cannot read properties of null` every time it ran. Confirmed by executing the old expression. They now get a `(no email — phone-only)` bucket, because that is the number the breakdown is read for |
| 🔴 The auth wordmark moved left and shrank | ✅ **v1.142.0.** v1.137.0 put the old `.auth__brand` class on `<app-logo>`, whose `:host` is `display: contents` — **an element with no box**, so `text-align: center` and `margin-bottom` styled nothing and the wordmark fell to the left edge of the card. The class is back on a real `<div>`; the rule is layout only, since the component owns the type. Reported from a phone. §70 |
| ⚠️ My v1.137.0 message said that wordmark was unstyled. It was not | **`styles.scss` has always carried `.auth &__brand`** with the size, the centring and the gap. I grepped for `auth__brand`, and SCSS nesting writes it `&__brand`. The dead `span { color: terracotta }` in it would have repainted both halves of the component wordmark and was inert only by specificity. §70 |
| 🔴 Every auth-page logo check was measuring the NAVBAR | ✅ **v1.142.0.** `readLogo(page, 0)` — and the navbar renders on the auth routes, so `app-logo` resolves to three hosts and index 0 is the header's. "MA is #C4522C on /auth/login" was true of a wordmark in the navbar and would have stayed green with the card's any colour at all. The route was in the loop; the subject never was. **The fifth form of this repo's recurring defect: right page, wrong element.** §70 |
| ⚠️ The phone shrink belonged to the nav row, not to the size | ✅ **v1.142.0.** The 480px rule is now `a.logo--lg` — the header, which must fit Log in and Get started on one line. The auth placement is alone in its card and was shrinking for nothing. Asserted both ways (auth 25.6px, header 20px and still an `<a>`), because deleting the rule would have fixed the auth pages and broken the nav row. §70 |
| ⚠️ A threshold that passed with the bug back | **The gap check was `>= 4` and the broken state measures 5px** — the title's own margin. The two states are 5px and 12px, so the line sits at 9px. Caught by reintroducing the defect, not by reading the code. §70 |
| ⚠️ The board can be searched by move-in date | ✅ **v1.146.0.** `availableFrom` has always existed and been required; nothing could search on it beyond one fortnight-wide boolean. A room now qualifies when it is free **on or before** the date asked for — a room free 25 November suits a 1 December move, one free 15 December does not — with an optional window for somebody flexible. §74. 🔴 **v1.146.1: it shipped reading `mm/dd/yyyy`.** A native `<input type="date">` renders in the **browser's** locale, not the page's — `index.html` has said `lang="en-ZA"` since the beginning and changes nothing — so on a browser set to en-US the most important field in the filter asked a South African for the month first, refused `25/11`, and showed `11/12` to somebody who could not tell which number was the day. The order cannot be set from the page at all, so the fix is not to format the box but to **echo the chosen date back in words beneath it** (`25 November 2026`), where the month is a name and no digit can be misread; the bound in `.filter-note` gained its year for the same reason. The drive builds the expected text by **splitting the ISO string** and indexing a literal month table rather than re-running the component's own date arithmetic, which would pass just as happily if that arithmetic were wrong in both places. Falsified by echoing the raw value: **2 failed**. ⚠️ Verified at 360/390/768/1280 — echo visible, input 44px, no horizontal spill — and the native box still reads `11/25/2026` in this container, which is the point: that is not fixable and no longer matters |
| ⚠️ The window is bound by its FAR end | **Somebody free any time in December will take a room that frees up on the 15th and one standing empty today.** A clause keyed on the window's start would have hidden every empty room, which is most of what they want to see. Reintroducing that keying fails the drive. §74 |
| ⚠️ A whole day, in both directions | `availableFrom` lands at **midnight UTC** (02:00 in Johannesburg), and a tenant asking for 1 December means any time on the 1st where they live. The comparison is against the last instant of that day at a fixed `+02:00` — not the server's UTC, not the browser's zone. SAST has no daylight saving, so the fixed offset is right all year. §74 |
| ⚠️ One availability clause, built once | `availableNow` and `moveIn` both constrain `availableFrom`, and two keys of the same name in one object literal keep only the last — the defect the household filters already carry a warning about. Combined into the tightest bound, and made exclusive on screen so a lit chip cannot misdescribe a search. §74 |
| ⚠️ Every card says WHEN, not just the immediate ones | "⚡ Available now" as before, "📅 From 25 November" on the rest. **No "date not confirmed" badge**: the field is required and the column NOT NULL, so that state cannot exist — building a label for it would be a branch nothing can reach. The drive asserts the refusal and reads `information_schema`. §74 |
| ⚠️ A date is a search criterion, not a booking | Nothing is reserved and no landlord is told; the drive asserts the room is unchanged after a search. A landlord moving the date reaches the board at once **without touching applications** — proven by applying, pushing the date back a month, and reading the application still `pending`. §74 |
| 🔴 Two faults in the drive, found by running it | A count comparison between two filters was comparing **capped pages** (50 vs 50, proving nothing) until every search was scoped to the run's own rooms; and the drawer opener was matched on the word "filter", which also finds "Clear all filters" inside the panel — clicking a button that cannot close the sheet. §74 |
| ⚠️ The contextual question is back, as its own workflow | ✅ **v1.145.0.** One question at one moment: a landlord who has just marked a room let is asked what is most stressful about tenants; a tenant who has just applied is asked when they need to move. It asks only a question that is unanswered, answering it is what stops it, and "not now" on one question does not silence the other six. §73 |
| 🔴 It would have shipped recording a 7-question survey as finished | **`submit()` marks a response complete**, and the card used it — so one contextual answer would have closed the set with six questions never asked, taking the inbox row with it. `submitMicro()` saves with `complete: false` and the answer merges into the same open set. §73 |
| 🔴 Two checks that could not see their subject, in one feature | The API-level check passed with the component reverted (**the component is the caller; the endpoint cannot see which call it makes**), and removing the "already answered" guard also stayed green because only the card decides to render nothing. The drive now answers the card in a browser and reads the database, then applies for a second room to prove it is not asked twice. §73 |
| 🔴 A tenant survey could not be stored at all | ✅ **v1.144.0.** `SurveyResponse` was keyed on `landlordProfileId` and `submit()` threw "Only a landlord account can answer this survey" — so the tenant half of this feature was not unbuilt, it was unstorable. Responses and dismissals are keyed on the **user** now and carry the role answered as, copied at answer time so a later role change cannot move old answers into the other group. §72 |
| ⚠️ Three things the old model could not express | **`completedAt`** (a row existed or it did not, so part-answered and finished were one state — nothing could resume and no completion rate existed), **`version`** (rewording a question in place changed what existing answers meant; new wording is a new row now), and a tenant survey. The migration backfills rather than drops, and fails loudly on a row it cannot map. §72 |
| ⚠️ The questions are asked at `/welcome`, after the account exists | **Sequence, not care.** Registration is one POST and this is the next screen, so a refresh, a skip or a closed tab cannot cost a sign-up. One question per step, every step saved on the SERVER, resumable on another device, every question optional and said so before the first. "Later" is a 30-day dismissal that keeps what was answered. §72 |
| ⚠️ The tenant questions are not the landlord ones reworded | Landlords are asked what is hard about a job they do; tenants what they are looking for and what goes wrong while they look. The drive asserts the two sets share **no question id**. No exact budget figure — a band answers the product question and an exact number on somebody looking for a home is sensitive for nothing (POPIA s.10). §72 |
| ⚠️ Both dashboard survey cards are gone | Replaced by ONE row at the bottom of the task inbox, under everything somebody is actually waiting on, which disappears when the set is finished or skipped. §72 |
| ⚠️ The admin screen's own comment came true | It hardcoded one slug behind "a second survey would need a picker; v1 does not build one". Now there is a picker, a date range, a landlord/tenant split never added together, a completion rate (null when nobody has started) and a CSV **of counts, never a row per person**. §72 |
| 🔴 `dbQuery` has been returning only the first line | **Found by a check that said "two answers" and printed one.** Every multi-row read in every drive has been seeing row one and reporting it as the whole answer. `dbRows` now exists beside it. §72 |
| 🔴 A room back on the board that refused every applicant | ✅ **v1.143.0.** `cancel()` archived the tenancy and reopened the room but left the application `accepted` and the letting cycle unmoved — and applications are unique per `(roomId, tenantId, cycle)`, so the accepted tenant got "You have already applied for this room" and everyone `autoRejectOthers` had rejected was locked out too. The cycle now ends with the letting: the application closes as `withdrawn` or `rejected` by **who** called it off, the rest of the cycle is archived, the counter moves. Nobody is silently reinstated; everyone can apply again. §71 |
| 🔴 The notices screen was empty for almost every account | ✅ **v1.143.0.** `NoticeRouter.deliver` returned as soon as an email was handed off, so no notice row was written for anybody with an email address — under a screen that says "This screen is the record". Worse: `NotificationsService.send` does not throw when email is unconfigured, it logs "[email skipped — no API key]" and resolves, so the person was told **nothing anywhere**. That is the reported "the tenant is not notified". The row is always written now; email is an extra channel. §71 |
| ⚠️ And that uncovered an invisible button | ✅ **v1.143.0.** "Mark all as read" only renders when something is unread, and nothing was ever unread because nothing was written. The moment notices existed, `a11y-drive` measured it at **1.00:1**: it used `.btn-ghost`, the navbar's cream-on-dark variant, on a cream page. `.btn-ghost-light` existed all along; `lease-documents.ts` had the same latent mistake. §41 all over again. §71 |
| ⚠️ A letting that never started is not a past letting | ✅ **v1.143.0.** The archive counted both kinds together, so a tenant who had rented one room read "Past lettings 2". Both records are kept — the called-off one now has its own section, count and a line saying no rent was ever owed. One row template serves both. §71 |
| ⚠️ Notices open one at a time, and fit the screen | ✅ **v1.143.0.** Every notice rendered expanded with body text past the edge of a phone. A real `<button>` head with `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` at 44px, body on tap, opening marks it read, and `min-width: 0` on the growing flex child is what stops the spill. §71 |
| 🔴 A drive reported 297 passed against a stale bundle | **Caught while reintroducing a bug on purpose.** The reverted code did not compile, `ng serve` kept serving the previous build, and the UI checks measured a screen whose source was broken. The drive now looks for the build-error overlay on every screen it measures, not only at sign-in. §71 |
| ✅ The applicant action row is covered now | ✅ **v1.141.0.** `a11y-drive.mjs` gained a `REVEAL` table — route pattern → a step run after the load and before any measurement — and expands the first applicant card. With the `white-space: normal` fix reverted it reports `Remove from shortlist` spilling **32px** and `Invite to view` 11px; before the reveal the same revert passed. §64's second card, closed. §69 |
| ⚠️ The first version of that check failed a correct screen | **Requiring the action row on every applicants route was wrong** — one fixture room exists to produce a finished tenancy, so its applicant is `accepted` and the row is correctly absent. The claim is now made once at the end: the row was on screen at least once, with a shortlist control. Driven to failure by pointing the counter at a class that does not exist. §69 |
| 🔴 A button nobody using voice control could speak | ✅ **v1.141.0.** Expanding the card brought `tenant-notes` under the audit for the first time and it failed **WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name**: `aria-label` read "Show your private notes about Thandi" over a button reading "Add a private note". Reachable since Phase 5e, and found by opening something rather than by reading code. §69 |
| ⚠️ Only the first card, on one route | **Recorded, not implied.** A defect needing two cards open, or living behind the references block, the invite form or the new outcome form, is still unmeasured. Each is one `REVEAL` entry. The three ratcheted overflow defects in `overflow-baseline.json` are also still unfixed. §69 |
| 🔴 A viewing that had been and gone still said "they are coming" | ✅ **v1.140.0.** `RoomViewing` had no vocabulary for an agreed time that had passed: the row stayed `accepted` for ever, the tenant dashboard said "✅ You said you are coming" about a Saturday weeks gone, the landlord card said "✅ They are coming", and **neither side was ever asked what became of it**. `ViewingOutcome` adds five answers, recordable once, by **either side**, only on a viewing both agreed to and only once its time has passed. The notice, the five answers and a reschedule option are on BOTH screens. §68 |
| ⚠️ "Found another tenant" accepts NOBODY | **The owner's own framing — it is an acceptance by another route in the landlord's head, and must not be one in the database.** `fell_through_other_tenant` closes that one application as `rejected` and stops. Accepting decides where a person lives and has a thirty-minute undo; inferring it from a dropdown would bypass that. The payload and the screen both name the two honest next steps (accept the person you chose, or pause the listing), and the drive asserts the other applicant is still `pending`, no tenancy exists, and the room is still on the board. §68 |
| ⚠️ The notice would have been a branch over a row that could not arrive | ✅ **v1.140.0.** `GET /viewings/mine` filtered `startsAt >= now` and the tenant panel is drawn from it — so the notice branched on a state **the API could never hand it**. Same shape in `invite()`: a past viewing still counted as "already arranged", so "offer another time" would have been a button that always failed. Both fixed; two times still to come is still refused. §68 |
| ⚠️ Driven to failure three ways, and the drive itself was wrong | **Backend reverted** → the list check and the reschedule check fail and the tenant panel renders nothing; **tenant branch removed** → all four text checks fail, the negative one included because each proves its subject first; **landlord branch removed** → `6 failed, 270 passed`. The negative runs also showed an unguarded `click()` **threw** and ended the run before the landlord half — a crash reported where a list of failures belonged. Both clicks are guarded by a count now. §68 |
| ⚠️ Nothing chases an elapsed viewing on a schedule | **Deliberate.** The notice is in both inboxes and on both dashboards and appears when somebody looks. A scheduled chase is a new job, and while `WHATSAPP_ENABLED` is false it would be a promise of a message nobody can be sent. §68 |
| ⚠️ The width check was given the subjects it lacked | ✅ **v1.139.0.** A long-titled room with a shortlisted applicant, and a long-titled room whose tenancy is `ended`. **The archive card is now covered and proven**: reverting its fix fails the drive with `arc-row__head wider than its box`. §67 |
| 🔴 The public room page scrolled sideways 171px | ✅ **v1.139.0.** Found on the fixture's first run. `.gallery__main` measured **563px in a 412px viewport**: `.gallery` is a grid, a grid item defaults to `min-width: auto` and will not shrink below its content's intrinsic size, so the hero image's natural width won the column — the img's own `width: 100%` cannot help. `min-width: 0` on the children; hero now 372px, page exactly 412. Nothing to do with the long title: it needs a room **with photos**, so this has presumably affected every such room |
| ⚠️ The applicant action row is still NOT covered | **Reverting its fix does not fail the drive.** The cards render collapsed, so `.applicant-card__actions` is not in the DOM on load — the drive would have to expand one first. Not a missing record this time, a missing interaction. Undone work |
| 🔴 A shortlist outlived its letting, on both sides | ✅ **v1.138.0.** "A landlord wants to meet you" sat above "None of your applications are still open", naming a room the same page listed as a past letting. The inbox's `shortlisted` branch had **no state check of any kind** while the `accepted` branch beside it had checked the tenancy since Phase D — so Phase D fixed the frontend's definition of "open" and left the backend's alone. The landlord side mirrored it: pending applicants on a `let`/`paused` room still read "waiting to hear from you". Both now ask what state the **room** is in. §66 |
| ⚠️ Three faults in the drive that proved it | **The first scenario passed without the fix** — accepting a rival applicant auto-rejects the shortlist, so `status` alone excluded it; it pauses the room now. **The first assertions were vacuous both ways** — the payload's `counts` map has a key per kind, so a string match over the body is always true. And **one check had been failing since Phase J1** (`.yard-past__review`, a class J1 deleted) with nothing acting on it |
| 🔴 The auth pages had their own wordmark | ✅ **v1.137.0.** `/auth/login` and `/auth/register` carried `Mas<span>tande</span>` — pre-Phase-I, split on the wrong letters, **styled by no rule anywhere**, so it inherited colours from whatever was nearby and put the terracotta on the wrong half. Phase I's requirement was "wherever the logo appears"; I converted the header and footer and shipped. §65 |
| ⚠️ `logo-drive.mjs` only ever loaded `/` | ✅ **v1.137.0.** Eighty-odd checks at four widths, closing with "one wordmark component" — a claim about everywhere, measured on one route. Now visits both auth routes too, and asserts **no wordmark is built from raw spans outside the component**, which is the check that catches a regression: putting the old markup back left the component-present check passing, because the header and footer still supply two hosts |
| 🔴 Two controls squashed by a global `nowrap` | ✅ **v1.136.0.** `:where(button)` sets `white-space: nowrap` — right for a short label, wrong for `.applicant-card__actions button` (two-up at ≤480px, "Remove from shortlist" spilling past its borders) and `.arc-row__head` (the whole archive card header is a button, so the room title clipped: pill read `TING`, `Reviews still open` read `ill open`). `white-space: normal` on both, `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the title. §64 |
| ⚠️ Nothing in the repo ever measured width | ✅ **v1.136.0.** CLAUDE.md requires checking horizontal scroll at four widths; `mobile-drive.mjs` is a regression suite for five named bugs and never measured it. `a11y-drive.mjs` now does, on all 40 pages, **per element** — neither reported defect produced a page-level scrollbar, so a document-level check would have passed on both. Found **three more**: `/` ad slot 4px (public), `/admin/advertising` **63px page scroll**, `/admin/analytics` 18px. Ratcheted in `overflow-baseline.json` |
| ⚠️ The width check does NOT cover the two cards reported | **Proven by reverting the fix and watching it pass.** The drive's account has no long-titled past letting and no shortlisted applicant, so neither card renders — §56's rule a third time, a check cannot see a subject the fixture never creates. Giving the drive those two records is undone work |
| 🔴 Shortlist and Invite-to-view were blank boxes on a phone | ✅ **v1.135.0.** `.applicant-card__actions button` set `background: var(--white)` and no `color`, so it kept `color: var(--white)` from the zero-specificity `:where(button)` — **white on white, 1.00:1**, emoji visible and words not. `.accept`/`.reject` set `color`, so the row looked like two missing labels rather than a CSS fault. Now `var(--ink)`: **17.94:1**. §63 |
| ⚠️ §41's contrast gate finally exists, after the defect shipped twice | ✅ **v1.135.0.** `control-contrast-audit.mjs`: a rule naming a button that sets a background must set a colour. Described as wanted-and-not-written in §41, and asserted-as-existing in a `screen-hint.ts` comment — so for several releases the only thing between that pairing and the next invisible control was somebody reading a sentence. Proven by reverting the fix; refuses to pass vacuously. In `npm run audit` and pre-commit |
| 🔴 The wordmark read "MAstande" for three days, and the drive required it | ✅ **v1.135.0.** Phase I's requirement — *"the MA portion must remain the same colour"* — is about colour; giving those letters their own element to pin `--logo-ma` was right, writing their contents as `MA` was not. **`logo-drive.mjs` asserted `'MAstande'`**, so a correct wordmark would have failed it: 76 checks green at four widths while the name was wrong in all eight. A check written from the implementation can only confirm the implementation. ⚠️ `aria-label` was `Mastande` throughout, so screen readers said it right while the eye read it wrong, and nothing compares the two. §62 |
| 🔴 Staging's `FRONTEND_URL` is the production site | **Found 13 October.** `https://www.umastande.co.za` on `mastande-api`. A Google sign-in on staging redirects to **production with the tokens in the fragment**; CORS admits only production so the real staging frontend cannot call its own API; and reference links sent to real people over WhatsApp point at production carrying a staging token. ⚠️ **Dashboard state — must be set by hand**, and nothing in the repo can detect it because `FRONTEND_URL` is `sync: false` by design. §61 |
| ⚠️ Three files gave three different answers for where staging is | ✅ **v1.134.0.** `environment.staging.ts` said `mastande.vercel.app` (**never existed** — the rebrand renamed the API service, not the Vercel project), `render.yaml` said `staging.umastande.co.za`, staging actually serves `rentboard-za1.vercel.app`. `SITE_URL` builds sitemap, robots.txt and every canonical, and the API starts fine with a wrong one, which is why it survived. Fixed in 5 code/config files and 7 docs. §60 |
| ⚠️ `env-parity` never compared `SITE_URL` to the frontend | ✅ **v1.134.0.** It checked `SITE_URL` was *declared* and that `APP_ENV` matched the branch, but never that it equalled the string the frontend builds — the one comparison that catches all three disagreements at once. Keyed on `APP_ENV`, not service name, so a rename cannot drop it. Driven to failure three ways |
| Production added to the Render blueprint | ✅ **v1.133.0.** `rentboard-api` existed nowhere in the repository, so nothing recorded that it tracked `develop`. Now a second service with `branch: master`, `APP_ENV=production`, `PAYFAST_SANDBOX=false`, and `JWT_SECRET` as `sync: false` rather than `generateValue` — a regenerated secret invalidates every live session. ⚠️ **Not synced yet: `plan: free` is a guess and must be confirmed, since declaring `free` on a paid instance downgrades it.** §59 |
| ⚠️ Adding it broke the gate that should have caught it | ✅ **v1.133.0.** `env-parity.mjs` grepped the whole file, so with two services `APP_ENV=production` (production block) paired with `branch: develop` (staging block) and it failed a correct file. Parses the YAML and checks each service against its own branch now, naming the offender.
