Driven to failure three ways — **including the exact misconfiguration that was live in the dashboard**, which it could not have caught before because the service was not in the file |
| ⚠️ The migration preflight called a healthy database unreachable | ✅ **v1.132.0.** Neon's pooled endpoint is PgBouncer, which **rejects** the `options` startup parameter; the preflight sets `PGOPTIONS=-c search_path=…`, so psql exited non-zero and it reported "❌ Cannot reach that database" about a fully-migrated production. A false negative on the check whose job is to say whether the target is sound, firing on the pooled string an operator actually pastes. Every query is already schema-qualified, so PGOPTIONS is dropped on pooler hosts. **Pooler path reasoned and unit-tested, not executed against Neon** — no network path from the container. §58 |
| ⚠️ Production deployed from `develop`, not `master` | ✅ **Fixed by the operator, 13 October — `rentboard-api` now tracks `master`.** Not verified from this container, which has no Render access; recorded as reported. **Found 13 October from the Render dashboard.** `rentboard-api` carries `APP_ENV=production` and the production Neon branch, and tracks **`develop`**. So a push to `master` deploys nothing, every push to `develop` goes straight to production, and staging and production receive the same commit at the same moment — staging gates nothing. `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md` says `master` → production; `render.yaml` line 46 says `branch: develop  # staging — the only environment Render serves`. The code and the dashboard disagreed and the dashboard won. ⚠️ **`render.yaml` still describes one service only** — the production service is not in the blueprint, so nothing in the repository records its branch, and the next person to read `render.yaml` will still conclude Render serves staging alone |
| 🔴 The house-ad fix did not converge, and announced that it had | ✅ **v1.131.0.** `seed-house-ads.ts` keys on fixed uuids, so a database seeded before those ids existed keeps its house ads under random ones: `--apply` created a correct **second** set beside the wrong one, six active house ads, and `ads.service.ts` ranks and serves the top — so the board still read "by RentBoard ZA" after a run reporting "✅ 3 house ads written". Verified by running the pre-fix file against that state: it printed **"Nothing to change — the house ads already match the code"** with a RentBoard advert live. House ads are now identified by `isHouseAd`, legacy rows are reported and **counted into `changes`** (the `if (!changes) return` is what hid the last one), and ended rather than deleted. §57 |
| ⚠️ Rehearse a data fix on production's SHAPE, not a fresh seed | **Both previous rounds were verified against a database seeded from current code — the one shape in which this bug cannot occur.** "Seeded before this id existed" is the commonest way a real database differs from a fresh one, and it is invisible to every check that builds its own fixture |
| ⚠️ Two POPIA checks were passing on leftover database state | ✅ **v1.130.0.** The directory must not ship `checkedByAdminId` or `sponsoredUntil` to a landlord, and both checks require `length>0` because each once passed on a 401 body — **but nothing in this repo had ever created a live provider**, so they were reading rows left by earlier drives. Green on this container's 3977-account database, red on a freshly seeded one. They now create their own subject through the admin route, or skip with the reason named when there is no `ADMIN_TOKEN`. Reproduced by rebuilding CI's database locally; proven by adding both columns back to the `select`. §56 |
| ⚠️ A check must create its subject, not inherit it | **The third form of the rule.** §54 was a check that could not fail; §55 and the fourteen before it were checks that could not see their subject; this is a check whose subject came from whatever happened to be in the database — a check of the database that reports on the code anyway. It had reported success for every release in which those two columns could have started leaking |
| ⚠️ Smoke §17f asserted a WhatsApp flow succeeds with WhatsApp off | ✅ **v1.130.0.** Assisted sign-up sends its code over WhatsApp and has no other channel, so the API correctly refuses with a 503 naming email; §17f asserted a flat 200. Same defect as the e2e spec and the eleven on-screen promises. §18 had been given two states for this exact reason and §17f was never brought in line. The off branch is **not a skip** — it asserts the 503, that it names email, and that the read-back is still a real array. Doctored the message to drop "email" and the check failed as intended |

## Migration required (v1.6.0)

The Application model gained `cycle` and `archivedAt`, and its unique
constraint changed from `[roomId, tenantId]` to `[roomId, tenantId, cycle]`.
Run before starting the API:

```bash
cd backend
npx prisma migrate dev --name application_letting_cycles
npx prisma generate
```

Existing applications default to `cycle = 0` and `archivedAt = null`, which
matches rooms that have never been relisted. Rooms with `relistCount > 0`
before this migration will show their historical applicants as belonging to
cycle 0 while the room is on a later cycle — those applicants simply drop off
the current list, which is the intended behaviour.
